===/home/lkhun9311/workspace/gpu-platform-control-plane/AGENTS.md
# gpu-platform-control-plane - AI Agent Guide

## Project Structure

**Single-group layout (default):**
```
cmd/main.go                    Manager entry (registers controllers/webhooks)
api/<version>/*_types.go       CRD schemas (+kubebuilder markers)
api/<version>/zz_generated.*   Auto-generated (DO NOT EDIT)
internal/controller/*          Reconciliation logic
internal/webhook/*             Validation/defaulting (if present)
config/crd/bases/*             Generated CRDs (DO NOT EDIT)
config/rbac/role.yaml          Generated RBAC (DO NOT EDIT)
config/samples/*               Example CRs (edit these)
Makefile                       Build/test/deploy commands
PROJECT                        Kubebuilder metadata Auto-generated (DO NOT EDIT)
```

**Multi-group layout** (for projects with multiple API groups):
```
api/<group>/<version>/*_types.go       CRD schemas by group
internal/controller/<group>/*          Controllers by group
internal/webhook/<group>/<version>/*   Webhooks by group and version (if present)
```

Multi-group layout organizes APIs by group name (e.g., `batch`, `apps`). Check the `PROJECT` file for `multigroup: true`.

**To convert to multi-group layout:**
1. Run: `kubebuilder edit --multigroup=true`
2. Move APIs: `mkdir -p api/<group> && mv api/<version> api/<group>/`
3. Move controllers: `mkdir -p internal/controller/<group> && mv internal/controller/*.go internal/controller/<group>/`
4. Move webhooks (if present): `mkdir -p internal/webhook/<group> && mv internal/webhook/<version> internal/webhook/<group>/`
5. Update import paths in all files
6. Fix `path` in `PROJECT` file for each resource
7. Update test suite CRD paths (add one more `..` to relative paths)

## Critical Rules

### Never Edit These (Auto-Generated)
- `config/crd/bases/*.yaml` - from `make manifests`
- `config/rbac/role.yaml` - from `make manifests`
- `config/webhook/generated/manifests.yaml` - from `make manifests`

**`config/webhook/manifests.yaml` is the exception: it is HAND-WRITTEN and safe to edit.** controller-gen
cannot express a `namespaceSelector`, which the Pod and Job guards need, so the deployed configuration is
maintained by hand and `make manifests` is pointed at `generated/` instead.

This list previously named that file as generated. Both statements lived in the repository at once -- this
one, and the file's own header saying it was hand-written -- and the file sat at controller-gen's default
output path, so every `make test` overwrote it and then tested the result. Four workflows were red.
`TestHandWrittenWebhookMatchesTheMarkers` now holds the hand-written file to the markers.
- `**/zz_generated.*.go` - from `make generate`
- `PROJECT` - from `kubebuilder [OPTIONS]`

### Never Remove Scaffold Markers
Do NOT delete `// +kubebuilder:scaffold:*` comments. CLI injects code at these markers.

### Keep Project Structure
Do not move files around. The CLI expects files in specific locations.

### Always Use CLI Commands
Always use `kubebuilder create api` and `kubebuilder create webhook` to scaffold. Do NOT create files manually.

### E2E Tests Require an Isolated Kind Cluster
The e2e tests are designed to validate the solution in an isolated environment (similar to GitHub Actions CI).
Ensure you run them against a dedicated [Kind](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/) cluster (not your “real” dev/prod cluster).

## After Making Changes

**After editing `*_types.go` or markers:**
```
make manifests  # Regenerate CRDs/RBAC from markers
make generate   # Regenerate DeepCopy methods
```

**After editing `*.go` files:**
```
make lint-fix   # Auto-fix code style
make test       # Run unit tests
```

## CLI Commands Cheat Sheet

### Create API (your own types)
```bash
kubebuilder create api --group <group> --version <version> --kind <Kind>
```

### Deploy Image Plugin (scaffold to deploy/manage ANY container image)

Generate a controller that deploys and manages a container image (nginx, redis, memcached, your app, etc.):

```bash
# Example: deploying memcached
kubebuilder create api --group example.com --version v1alpha1 --kind Memcached \
  --image=memcached:alpine \
  --plugins=deploy-image.go.kubebuilder.io/v1-alpha
```

Scaffolds good-practice code: reconciliation logic, status conditions, finalizers, RBAC. Use as a reference implementation.


### Create Webhooks
```bash
# Validation + defaulting
kubebuilder create webhook --group <group> --version <version> --kind <Kind> \
  --defaulting --programmatic-validation

# Conversion webhook (for multi-version APIs)
kubebuilder create webhook --group <group> --version v1 --kind <Kind> \
  --conversion --spoke v2
```

### Controller for Core Kubernetes Types
```bash
# Watch Pods
kubebuilder create api --group core --version v1 --kind Pod \
  --controller=true --resource=false

# Watch Deployments
kubebuilder create api --group apps --version v1 --kind Deployment \
  --controller=true --resource=false
```

### Controller for External Types (e.g., from other operators)

Watch resources from external APIs (cert-manager, Argo CD, Istio, etc.):

```bash
# Example: watching cert-manager Certificate resources
kubebuilder create api \
  --group cert-manager --version v1 --kind Certificate \
  --controller=true --resource=false \
  --external-api-path=github.com/cert-manager/cert-manager/pkg/apis/certmanager/v1 \
  --external-api-domain=io \
  --external-api-module=github.com/cert-manager/cert-manager
```

**Note:** Use `--external-api-module=<module>@<version>` only if you need a specific version. Otherwise, omit `@<version>` to use what's in go.mod.

### Webhook for External Types

```bash
# Example: validating external resources
kubebuilder create webhook \
  --group cert-manager --version v1 --kind Issuer \
  --defaulting \
  --external-api-path=github.com/cert-manager/cert-manager/pkg/apis/certmanager/v1 \
  --external-api-domain=io \
  --external-api-module=github.com/cert-manager/cert-manager
```

## Testing & Development

```bash
make test              # Run unit tests (uses envtest: real K8s API + etcd)
make run               # Run locally (uses current kubeconfig context)
```

Tests use **Ginkgo + Gomega** (BDD style). Check `suite_test.go` for setup.

## Deployment Workflow

```bash
# 1. Regenerate manifests
make manifests generate

# 2. Build & deploy
export IMG=<registry>/<project>:tag
make docker-build docker-push IMG=$IMG  # Or: kind load docker-image $IMG --name <cluster>
make deploy IMG=$IMG

# 3. Test
kubectl apply -k config/samples/

# 4. Debug
kubectl logs -n <project>-system deployment/<project>-controller-manager -c manager -f
```

### API Design

**Key markers for** `api/<version>/*_types.go`:

```go
// +kubebuilder:object:root=true
// +kubebuilder:subresource:status
// +kubebuilder:resource:scope=Namespaced
// +kubebuilder:printcolumn:name="Status",type=string,JSONPath=".status.conditions[?(@.type=='Ready')].status"

// On fields:
// +kubebuilder:validation:Required
// +kubebuilder:validation:Minimum=1
// +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=100
// +kubebuilder:validation:Pattern="^[a-z]+$"
// +kubebuilder:default="value"
```

- **Use** `metav1.Condition` for status (not custom string fields)
- **Use predefined types**: `metav1.Time` instead of `string` for dates
- **Follow K8s API conventions**: Standard field names (`spec`, `status`, `metadata`)

### Controller Design

**RBAC markers in** `internal/controller/*_controller.go`:

```go
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=mygroup.example.com,resources=mykinds,verbs=get;list;watch;create;update;patch;delete
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=mygroup.example.com,resources=mykinds/status,verbs=get;update;patch
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=mygroup.example.com,resources=mykinds/finalizers,verbs=update
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=events.k8s.io,resources=events,verbs=create;patch
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=apps,resources=deployments,verbs=get;list;watch;create;update;patch;delete
```

**Implementation rules:**
- **Idempotent reconciliation**: Safe to run multiple times
- **Re-fetch before updates**: `r.Get(ctx, req.NamespacedName, obj)` before `r.Update` to avoid conflicts
- **Structured logging**: `log := log.FromContext(ctx); log.Info("msg", "key", val)`
- **Owner references**: Enable automatic garbage collection (`SetControllerReference`)
- **Watch secondary resources**: Use `.Owns()` or `.Watches()`, not just `RequeueAfter`
- **Finalizers**: Clean up external resources (buckets, VMs, DNS entries)

### Logging

**Follow Kubernetes logging message style guidelines:**

- Start from a capital letter
- Do not end the message with a period
- Active voice: subject present (`"Deployment could not create Pod"`) or omitted (`"Could not create Pod"`)
- Past tense: `"Could not delete Pod"` not `"Cannot delete Pod"`
- Specify object type: `"Deleted Pod"` not `"Deleted"`
- Balanced key-value pairs

```go
log.Info("Starting reconciliation")
log.Info("Created Deployment", "name", deploy.Name)
log.Error(err, "Failed to create Pod", "name", name)
```

**Reference:** https://github.com/kubernetes/community/blob/master/contributors/devel/sig-instrumentation/logging.md#message-style-guidelines

### Webhooks
- **Create all types together**: `--defaulting --programmatic-validation --conversion`
- **When`--force`is used**: Backup custom logic first, then restore after scaffolding
- **For multi-version APIs**: Use hub-and-spoke pattern (`--conversion --spoke v2`)
  - Hub version: Usually oldest stable version (v1)
  - Spoke versions: Newer versions that convert to/from hub (v2, v3)
  - Example: `--group crew --version v1 --kind Captain --conversion --spoke v2` (v1 is hub, v2 is spoke)

### Learning from Examples

The **deploy-image plugin** scaffolds a complete controller following good practices. Use it as a reference implementation:

```bash
kubebuilder create api --group example --version v1alpha1 --kind MyApp \
  --image=<your-image> --plugins=deploy-image.go.kubebuilder.io/v1-alpha
```

Generated code includes: status conditions (`metav1.Condition`), finalizers, owner references, events, idempotent reconciliation.

## Distribution Options

### Option 1: YAML Bundle (Kustomize)

```bash
# Generate dist/install.yaml from Kustomize manifests
make build-installer IMG=<registry>/<project>:tag
```

**Key points:**
- The `dist/install.yaml` is generated from Kustomize manifests (CRDs, RBAC, Deployment)
- Commit this file to your repository for easy distribution
- Users only need `kubectl` to install (no additional tools required)

**Example:** Users install with a single command:
```bash
kubectl apply -f https://raw.githubusercontent.com/<org>/<repo>/<tag>/dist/install.yaml
```

### Option 2: Helm Chart

```bash
kubebuilder edit --plugins=helm/v2-alpha                      # Generates dist/chart/ (default)
kubebuilder edit --plugins=helm/v2-alpha --output-dir=charts  # Generates charts/chart/
```

**For development:**
```bash
make helm-deploy IMG=<registry>/<project>:<tag>          # Deploy manager via Helm
make helm-deploy IMG=$IMG HELM_EXTRA_ARGS="--set ..."    # Deploy with custom values
make helm-status                                         # Show release status
make helm-uninstall                                      # Remove release
make helm-history                                        # View release history
make helm-rollback                                       # Rollback to previous version
```

**For end users/production:**
```bash
helm install my-release ./<output-dir>/chart/ --namespace <ns> --create-namespace
```

**Important:** If you add webhooks or modify manifests after initial chart generation:
1. Backup any customizations in `<output-dir>/chart/values.yaml` and `<output-dir>/chart/manager/manager.yaml`
2. Re-run: `kubebuilder edit --plugins=helm/v2-alpha --force` (use same `--output-dir` if customized)
3. Manually restore your custom values from the backup

### Publish Container Image

```bash
export IMG=<registry>/<project>:<version>
make docker-build docker-push IMG=$IMG
```

+	// gpuFaultScrapeTotal counts the DCGM path's exporter scrapes, by result.
+	//
+	// A fault path whose scrapes all fail reports no fault, and without this series that would read as a
+	// fleet of healthy cards; the result label is the reason the GPUSignal condition carries.
+	gpuFaultScrapeTotal = promauto.With(metrics.Registry).NewCounterVec(
+		prometheus.CounterOpts{
+			Name: metricPrefix + "gpufault_scrape_total",
+			Help: "DCGM exporter scrapes by the GPU fault path, labeled by result (ok or the failure reason).",
+		},
+		[]string{"result"},
+	)
+
+	// gpuFaultTransitionsTotal counts faults latched, by the condition reason, and latches released ("Cleared").
+	gpuFaultTransitionsTotal = promauto.With(metrics.Registry).NewCounterVec(
+		prometheus.CounterOpts{
+			Name: metricPrefix + "gpufault_transitions_total",
+			Help: "GPU faults latched on a NodeHealth, labeled by reason, and latches cleared (reason Cleared).",
+		},
+		[]string{"reason"},
+	)
+

+	gpuFaultWorkloadActionsTotal = promauto.With(metrics.Registry).NewCounterVec(
+		prometheus.CounterOpts{
+			Name: metricPrefix + "gpufault_workload_actions_total",
+			Help: "Kueue Workloads displaced from a GPU-faulted node, labeled by action (deactivated, reactivated).",
+		},
+		[]string{"action"},
+	)
+
+	// gpuFaultObserveToTaintSeconds is the time from the scrape that first carried a fault to the taint existing.
+	//

package controller

import (
	"flag"
	"fmt"
	"time"

	"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/labels"
	ctrl "sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/event"
)

// GPUFaultFlags are the operator flags of the DCGM fault path.
//
// Every one is inert until Source is "dcgm": the path is opt-in, because a platform that taints nodes on a
// signal its operator never deployed would be quarantining on the absence of an exporter.
type GPUFaultFlags struct {
	Source         string
	Namespace      string
	Selector       string
	Port           int
	Interval       time.Duration
	Timeout        time.Duration
	StaleAfter     time.Duration
	EvictWorkloads bool
}

// Bind registers the flags. The defaults are the registration's (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-gpu-fault-reaction-design.md):
// a 1 s scrape matching the exporter's 1 s collection, a 0.8 s timeout, and stale after 5 s.
func (f *GPUFaultFlags) Bind(fs *flag.FlagSet) {
	fs.StringVar(&f.Source, "gpu-fault-source", "",
		`Where GPU fault signals come from: "" (off) or "dcgm" (scrape each node's DCGM exporter).`)
	fs.StringVar(&f.Namespace, "gpu-fault-exporter-namespace", "gpu-platform-control-plane-system",
		"Namespace of the DCGM exporter Pods.")
	fs.StringVar(&f.Selector, "gpu-fault-exporter-selector", "app.kubernetes.io/component=dcgm-exporter",
		"Label selector of the DCGM exporter Pods; exactly one Running, Ready Pod per node is read.")
	fs.IntVar(&f.Port, "gpu-fault-exporter-port", 9400, "The exporter's metrics port on its Pod IP.")
	fs.DurationVar(&f.Interval, "gpu-fault-scrape-interval", time.Second, "How often each node's exporter is scraped.")
	fs.DurationVar(&f.Timeout, "gpu-fault-scrape-timeout", 800*time.Millisecond,
		"How long one scrape may take; must be shorter than the interval.")
	fs.DurationVar(&f.StaleAfter, "gpu-fault-stale-after", 5*time.Second,
		"How old the last successful scrape may be before the GPU signal is not fresh; must exceed the interval.")
	fs.BoolVar(&f.EvictWorkloads, "gpu-fault-evict-workloads", false,
		"Deactivate, and later reactivate, the Kueue Workloads of GPU Pods on a node with a latched GPU fault.")
}

// Setup builds the path from the flags and adds its scraper to the manager.
// It returns nil, and adds nothing, when the path is off.
func (f *GPUFaultFlags) Setup(mgr ctrl.Manager) (*GPUFaultConfig, error) {
	switch f.Source {
	case "":
		if f.EvictWorkloads {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("--gpu-fault-evict-workloads needs --gpu-fault-source; nothing would ever be evicted")
		}
		return nil, nil
	case "dcgm":
	default:
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("--gpu-fault-source=%q: the only source is dcgm", f.Source)
	}
	sel, err := labels.Parse(f.Selector)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("--gpu-fault-exporter-selector: %w", err)
	}
	cfg := GPUFaultSourceConfig{
		Namespace: f.Namespace, Selector: sel, Port: f.Port,
		Interval: f.Interval, Timeout: f.Timeout, StaleAfter: f.StaleAfter,
	}
	if err := cfg.Validate(); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	store := NewGPUFaultStore()
	// Buffered so that one slow reconcile does not stall the scrape of every other node.
	events := make(chan event.GenericEvent, 1024)
	src := &GPUFaultSource{
		Config: cfg, Reader: mgr.GetClient(), PodReader: mgr.GetAPIReader(), Store: store, Events: events,
	}
	if err := mgr.Add(src); err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("add gpu fault source: %w", err)
	}
	return &GPUFaultConfig{
		Store: store, StaleAfter: f.StaleAfter, EvictWorkloads: f.EvictWorkloads,
		APIReader: mgr.GetAPIReader(), Recorder: mgr.GetEventRecorder("nodehealth-gpufault"), Events: events,
	}, nil
}

package controller

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"strconv"
	"sync"
	"time"

	corev1 "k8s.io/api/core/v1"
	"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/labels"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/client"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/event"
	logf "sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/log"

	platformv1 "github.com/lkhun9311/gpu-mlops-platform-control-plane/api/v1"
	"github.com/lkhun9311/gpu-mlops-platform-control-plane/internal/gpufault"
)

// Reasons the GPUSignal condition carries when a node's exporter could not be read.
const (
	signalReasonFresh         = "Fresh"
	signalReasonNoObservation = "NoObservation"
	signalReasonStale         = "Stale"
	signalReasonNoExporter    = "NoExporter"
	signalReasonAmbiguous     = "AmbiguousExporter"
	signalReasonScrapeFailed  = "ScrapeFailed"
	signalReasonNoCards       = "NoCards"
)

// GPUFaultObservation is the last thing the scraper learned about one node.
type GPUFaultObservation struct {
	// LastSuccess is when the last scrape that could be assessed returned; zero if none has.
	LastSuccess time.Time
	// Assessment is that scrape's policy reading; it is meaningless when LastSuccess is zero.
	Assessment gpufault.Assessment
	// FaultSince is when the first scrape carrying the current non-empty fingerprint returned.
	//
	// It is the time the platform first knew of this fault, and the start of every hop it performs.
	FaultSince time.Time
	// ReadingSince is when the first scrape carrying the current reading, every value included, returned.
	ReadingSince time.Time
	// LastFailure and FailureReason describe the most recent attempt that failed, if it is newer than LastSuccess.
	LastFailure   time.Time
	FailureReason string
	FailureDetail string
}

// GPUFaultStore holds one observation per node, written by the scraper and read by the reconciler.
//
// It is in memory on purpose: everything the reconciler concludes from it is written to the NodeHealth and the
// Node, so a restarted operator starts from what it recorded rather than from what it remembered, and an
// observation that died with the process is replaced by the next scrape.
type GPUFaultStore struct {
	mu    sync.RWMutex
	nodes map[string]GPUFaultObservation
}

// NewGPUFaultStore returns an empty store.
func NewGPUFaultStore() *GPUFaultStore {
	return &GPUFaultStore{nodes: map[string]GPUFaultObservation{}}
}

// Get returns the node's observation, and whether there is one.
func (s *GPUFaultStore) Get(node string) (GPUFaultObservation, bool) {
	s.mu.RLock()
	defer s.mu.RUnlock()
	o, ok := s.nodes[node]
	return o, ok
}

// recordSuccess stores an assessed scrape and reports whether the fingerprint changed.
func (s *GPUFaultStore) recordSuccess(node string, at time.Time, a gpufault.Assessment) bool {
	s.mu.Lock()
	defer s.mu.Unlock()
	o := s.nodes[node]
	before := o.Assessment.Fingerprint()
	readingBefore := o.Assessment.Reading()
	fp := a.Fingerprint()
	switch {
	case fp == "":
		o.FaultSince = time.Time{}
	case fp != before || o.FaultSince.IsZero():
		o.FaultSince = at
	}
	if a.Reading() != readingBefore || o.ReadingSince.IsZero() {
		o.ReadingSince = at
	}
	o.Assessment = a
	o.LastSuccess = at
	s.nodes[node] = o
	// Freshness changes are the caller's to notice, tick by tick; this reports only a change in what was read.
	// The reading names every field, so a fault, a missing field and a benign change all count.
	return a.Reading() != readingBefore
}

// recordFailure stores a failed attempt; the last successful assessment is kept, and its age decides staleness.
// It reports whether this failure starts a run of failures or changes their reason, which is when it is logged.
func (s *GPUFaultStore) recordFailure(node string, at time.Time, reason, detail string) bool {
	s.mu.Lock()
	defer s.mu.Unlock()
	o := s.nodes[node]
	transition := !o.LastFailure.After(o.LastSuccess) || o.FailureReason != reason
	o.LastFailure = at
	o.FailureReason = reason
	o.FailureDetail = detail
	s.nodes[node] = o
	return transition
}

// failing reports whether the node's most recent attempt failed.
func (s *GPUFaultStore) failing(node string) bool {
	s.mu.RLock()
	defer s.mu.RUnlock()
	o, ok := s.nodes[node]
	return ok && o.LastFailure.After(o.LastSuccess)
}

// GPUFaultSourceConfig is how the operator finds and reads each node's DCGM exporter.
type GPUFaultSourceConfig struct {
	// Namespace and Selector find the exporter Pods; one Running, Ready Pod per node is required.
	Namespace string
	Selector  labels.Selector
	// Port is the exporter's metrics port on the Pod IP.
	Port int
	// Interval is the scrape period; the registration sets it equal to the exporter's 1 s collection period,
	// because scraping faster reads the same cached snapshot again.
	Interval time.Duration
	// Timeout bounds one scrape; it must be shorter than Interval or scrapes would overlap.
	Timeout time.Duration
	// StaleAfter is how old the last successful scrape may be before the signal is no longer fresh.
	StaleAfter time.Duration
}

// Validate refuses a configuration the reconciler's guarantees do not hold under.
func (c GPUFaultSourceConfig) Validate() error {
	switch {
	case c.Namespace == "":
		return fmt.Errorf("gpu fault source: no exporter namespace")
	case c.Selector == nil || c.Selector.Empty():
		return fmt.Errorf("gpu fault source: an empty exporter selector would match every Pod in %s", c.Namespace)
	case c.Port <= 0 || c.Port > 65535:
		return fmt.Errorf("gpu fault source: exporter port %d", c.Port)
	case c.Interval <= 0 || c.Timeout <= 0:
		return fmt.Errorf("gpu fault source: interval %s and timeout %s must both be positive", c.Interval, c.Timeout)
	case c.Timeout >= c.Interval:
		return fmt.Errorf("gpu fault source: timeout %s is not shorter than the interval %s", c.Timeout, c.Interval)
	case c.StaleAfter <= c.Interval:
		// One missed scrape must not read as stale, or every slow scrape would flap the signal.
		return fmt.Errorf("gpu fault source: stale-after %s must exceed the interval %s", c.StaleAfter, c.Interval)
	}
	return nil
}

// GPUFaultSource scrapes every NodeHealth's node's exporter on a fixed period and wakes the reconciler when
// what it knows about a node changes.
type GPUFaultSource struct {
	Config GPUFaultSourceConfig
	// Reader lists NodeHealths; the manager's cached client is fine for that.
	Reader client.Reader
	// PodReader lists exporter Pods. It is the uncached API reader, so that the operator does not start a
	// cluster-wide Pod informer to find a handful of DaemonSet Pods.
	PodReader client.Reader
	Store     *GPUFaultStore
	// Events receives one GenericEvent per NodeHealth to reconcile; the reconciler's controller watches it.
	Events chan event.GenericEvent
	// HTTP is the client scrapes use; nil means a client with Config.Timeout.
	HTTP *http.Client
	// Now is the clock; nil means time.Now.
	Now func() time.Time

	// lastFresh remembers each node's freshness at the previous tick, so a change of it wakes the reconciler.
	lastFresh map[string]bool
}

// NeedLeaderElection is true: two operators scraping and tainting at once would race each other's patches.
func (s *GPUFaultSource) NeedLeaderElection() bool { return true }

func (s *GPUFaultSource) now() time.Time {
	if s.Now != nil {
		return s.Now()
	}
	return time.Now()
}

// Start runs the scrape loop until the context ends.
func (s *GPUFaultSource) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
	if err := s.Config.Validate(); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if s.HTTP == nil {
		s.HTTP = &http.Client{Timeout: s.Config.Timeout}
	}
	s.lastFresh = map[string]bool{}
	t := time.NewTicker(s.Config.Interval)
	defer t.Stop()
	for {
		s.Tick(ctx)
		select {
		case <-ctx.Done():
			return nil
		case <-t.C:
		}
	}
}

// Tick scrapes every node once and wakes the reconciler for each node whose state changed.
//
// Exported so tests can drive the loop one step at a time without a ticker.
func (s *GPUFaultSource) Tick(ctx context.Context) {
	log := logf.FromContext(ctx).WithName("gpu-fault-source")
	if s.lastFresh == nil {
		s.lastFresh = map[string]bool{}
	}
	var nhs platformv1.NodeHealthList
	if err := s.Reader.List(ctx, &nhs); err != nil {
		log.Error(err, "Could not list NodeHealth resources; no node is scraped this tick")
		return
	}
	exporters, err := s.exportersByNode(ctx)
	for i := range nhs.Items {
		nh := &nhs.Items[i]
		node := nh.Spec.NodeName
		changed := s.scrapeNode(ctx, node, exporters, err)
		fresh := s.fresh(node)
		if prev, seen := s.lastFresh[node]; !seen || prev != fresh {
			changed = true
		}
		s.lastFresh[node] = fresh
		if changed {
			// A send that would block is dropped rather than stalling every other node's scrape: the next
			// change, or the next freshness transition, sends again, and the reconciler also runs on every
			// Node heartbeat.
			select {
			case s.Events <- event.GenericEvent{Object: &platformv1.NodeHealth{ObjectMeta: nh.ObjectMeta}}:
			default:
				log.Info("Reconcile wake-up dropped; the queue is full", "nodehealth", nh.Name)
			}
		}
	}
}

// fresh reports whether the node's last successful scrape is young enough to act on.
func (s *GPUFaultSource) fresh(node string) bool {
	o, ok := s.Store.Get(node)
	return ok && !o.LastSuccess.IsZero() && s.now().Sub(o.LastSuccess) <= s.Config.StaleAfter
}

// exportersByNode maps each node to the IPs of its Running, Ready exporter Pods.
func (s *GPUFaultSource) exportersByNode(ctx context.Context) (map[string][]string, error) {
	var pods corev1.PodList
	if err := s.PodReader.List(ctx, &pods, client.InNamespace(s.Config.Namespace),
		client.MatchingLabelsSelector{Selector: s.Config.Selector}); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	out := map[string][]string{}
	for i := range pods.Items {
		p := &pods.Items[i]
		if p.Spec.NodeName == "" || p.Status.PodIP == "" || p.Status.Phase != corev1.PodRunning || !podReady(p) ||
			p.DeletionTimestamp != nil {
			continue
		}
		out[p.Spec.NodeName] = append(out[p.Spec.NodeName], p.Status.PodIP)
	}
	return out, nil
}

func podReady(p *corev1.Pod) bool {
	for _, c := range p.Status.Conditions {
		if c.Type == corev1.PodReady {
			return c.Status == corev1.ConditionTrue
		}
	}
	return false
}

// scrapeNode reads one node's exporter and records the outcome; it reports whether the reconciler should run.
func (s *GPUFaultSource) scrapeNode(ctx context.Context, node string, exporters map[string][]string, listErr error) bool {
	log := logf.FromContext(ctx).WithName("gpu-fault-source")
	// Failures are logged when a run of them starts or changes reason, not on every tick: a node with no
	// exporter would otherwise log once a second for as long as it exists. The log is the evidence that a
	// detection was slow because a scrape failed, rather than because the mechanism is other than described.
	fail := func(reason, detail string) bool {
		if s.Store.recordFailure(node, s.now(), reason, detail) {
			log.Info("GPU fault scrape failing", "node", node, "reason", reason, "detail", detail)
		}
		gpuFaultScrapeTotal.WithLabelValues(reason).Inc()
		return false
	}
	if listErr != nil {
		return fail(signalReasonNoExporter, "listing exporter Pods failed: "+listErr.Error())
	}
	ips := exporters[node]
	switch len(ips) {
	case 0:
		return fail(signalReasonNoExporter, fmt.Sprintf("no Running, Ready Pod matching %s in %s on this node",
			s.Config.Selector, s.Config.Namespace))
	case 1:
	default:
		// Two exporters on one node could disagree, and choosing one would let the wrong one decide.
		return fail(signalReasonAmbiguous, fmt.Sprintf("%d exporter Pods on this node", len(ips)))
	}
	url := "http://" + net.JoinHostPort(ips[0], strconv.Itoa(s.Config.Port)) + "/metrics"
	sctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, s.Config.Timeout)
	defer cancel()
	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(sctx, http.MethodGet, url, nil)
	if err != nil {
		return fail(signalReasonScrapeFailed, err.Error())
	}
	resp, err := s.HTTP.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		return fail(signalReasonScrapeFailed, err.Error())
	}
	body, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 8<<20))
	_ = resp.Body.Close()
	if err != nil {
		return fail(signalReasonScrapeFailed, "read body: "+err.Error())
	}
	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
		return fail(signalReasonScrapeFailed, fmt.Sprintf("HTTP %d from %s", resp.StatusCode, url))
	}
	// The receipt time, not the request time: it is the earliest moment the platform could know the value.
	at := s.now()
	readings, err := gpufault.ParseExposition(body)
	if err != nil {
		return fail(signalReasonScrapeFailed, err.Error())
	}
	a, err := gpufault.Assess(readings)
	if err != nil {
		return fail(signalReasonScrapeFailed, err.Error())
	}
	if a.Cards == 0 {
		// An exporter that serves none of the fields has said nothing about the cards, which is not health.
		return fail(signalReasonNoCards, "the scrape carried none of the fault fields for any card")
	}
	gpuFaultScrapeTotal.WithLabelValues("ok").Inc()
	if s.Store.failing(node) {
		log.Info("GPU fault scrape recovered", "node", node)
	}
	return s.Store.recordSuccess(node, at, a)
}

package controller

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"slices"
	"time"

	corev1 "k8s.io/api/core/v1"
	"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/api/meta"
	metav1 "k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/apis/meta/v1"
	"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/runtime"
	"k8s.io/client-go/tools/events"
	"k8s.io/utils/ptr"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/client"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/event"
	logf "sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/log"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/reconcile"
	kueuev1beta1 "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/apis/kueue/v1beta1"

	platformv1 "github.com/lkhun9311/gpu-mlops-platform-control-plane/api/v1"
)

// gpuFaultTaintKey is the taint a DCGM-reported GPU fault puts on its node.
//
// It is a key of its own rather than the readiness taint, because the readiness path removes its taint
// whenever the kubelet is Ready, and a card can be faulted on a node whose kubelet is perfectly healthy.
const gpuFaultTaintKey = "platform.lkhun9311.github.io/gpu-fault"

// The annotations that carry the platform's own timestamps and the fault it has latched.
//
// They are annotations rather than status fields so that the API does not change for a study, and they hold
// RFC 3339 times with nanoseconds because a condition's lastTransitionTime is truncated to the second, which
// is coarser than the hops being measured.
const (
	gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix = "gpufault.platform.lkhun9311.github.io/"
	// annGPUFaultFingerprint is the latched fault; its presence, not the condition, is what survives a restart.
	annGPUFaultFingerprint = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "fingerprint"
	// annGPUFaultObservedAt is when the first scrape carrying the latched fingerprint returned.
	annGPUFaultObservedAt = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "observed-at"
	// annGPUFaultTaintedAt is written after the Node patch that added the taint succeeded.
	annGPUFaultTaintedAt = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "tainted-at"
	// annGPUFaultClearedAt is when a fresh scrape without the fault, or an acknowledgement, released the latch.
	annGPUFaultClearedAt = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "cleared-at"
	// annGPUFaultAcknowledged is written by an operator: a fingerprint here is not a fault any more.
	annGPUFaultAcknowledged = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "acknowledged"
	// annGPUFaultReading is every value the last fresh scrape carried, written only when it changes.
	//
	// It is the evidence that a value the policy ignores was read and ignored rather than never read: a quiet
	// control proves nothing about an observer that had stopped observing.
	annGPUFaultReading = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "reading"
	// annGPUFaultReadingAt is when the first scrape carrying that reading returned.
	annGPUFaultReadingAt = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "reading-at"

	// annWorkloadDeactivatedAt is when this platform sent the patch deactivating a Workload.
	annWorkloadDeactivatedAt = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "deactivated-at"
	// annWorkloadDeactivatedNode names the faulted node; only Workloads carrying it are ever reactivated.
	annWorkloadDeactivatedNode = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "deactivated-node"
	// annWorkloadFingerprint is the fault that displaced the Workload.
	annWorkloadFingerprint = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "deactivated-fingerprint"
	// annWorkloadReactivatedAt is when this platform sent the patch reactivating it.
	annWorkloadReactivatedAt = gpuFaultAnnotationPrefix + "reactivated-at"
)

// The two conditions the DCGM path adds to NodeHealth.
const (
	// conditionGPUSignal says whether the platform has a fresh reading of the node's cards at all.
	conditionGPUSignal = "GPUSignal"
	// conditionGPUHealthy says what that reading means; Unknown whenever it cannot say.
	conditionGPUHealthy = "GPUHealthy"

	gpuHealthyReasonNoFault        = "NoFault"
	gpuHealthyReasonAcknowledged   = "Acknowledged"
	gpuHealthyReasonSignalNotFresh = "SignalNotFresh"
	gpuHealthyReasonFieldMissing   = "FieldMissing"
	// gpuHealthyReasonLatched is used only when a fault is latched and the condition that named it is gone.
	gpuHealthyReasonLatched = "FaultLatched"
)

// faultSourceDCGM is the faultSignal source while a node is quarantined for a DCGM-reported fault.
const faultSourceDCGM = "dcgm"

// gpuResourceName is the extended resource whose Pods are displaced from a faulted node.
const gpuResourceName corev1.ResourceName = "nvidia.com/gpu"

// GPUFaultConfig turns on the DCGM fault path; a nil one on the reconciler leaves it exactly as it was.
type GPUFaultConfig struct {
	Store *GPUFaultStore
	// StaleAfter must be the source's, so that the reconciler and the scraper agree on what fresh means.
	StaleAfter time.Duration
	// EvictWorkloads lets the reconciler deactivate the Kueue Workloads of GPU Pods on a faulted node.
	EvictWorkloads bool
	// APIReader lists a node's Pods without a cluster-wide Pod informer.
	APIReader client.Reader
	// Recorder may be nil, in which case no events are emitted.
	Recorder events.EventRecorder
	// Events is the scraper's wake-up channel; the controller watches it.
	Events chan event.GenericEvent
	// Now is the clock; nil means time.Now.
	Now func() time.Time
}

func (c *GPUFaultConfig) now() time.Time {
	if c.Now != nil {
		return c.Now()
	}
	return time.Now()
}

func (c *GPUFaultConfig) event(obj runtime.Object, eventtype, reason, action, note string, args ...any) {
	if c.Recorder != nil {
		c.Recorder.Eventf(obj, nil, eventtype, reason, action, note, args...)
	}
}

// gpuFaultDecision is what one observation means for one node.
type gpuFaultDecision struct {
	signal  metav1.Condition
	healthy metav1.Condition
	// latch is the fingerprint to hold after this reconcile, "" for none.
	latch string
	// newLatch is true when latch is a fingerprint that was not held before.
	newLatch bool
	// observedAt is when the scraper first saw latch; set only with newLatch.
	observedAt time.Time
	// taint is +1 to ensure the taint, -1 to remove it, 0 to leave it as it is.
	taint int
	// reading and readingAt are the fresh scrape's values and when they first appeared; empty when not fresh.
	reading   string
	readingAt time.Time
}

// decideGPUFault applies the rules the registration states, and nothing else:
// a fresh fault latches and taints; a fresh reading whose fault fields all read zero, or that carries exactly
// the acknowledged fault, releases the latch; anything that is not fresh, or not complete, changes neither the
// latch nor the taint.
func decideGPUFault(obs GPUFaultObservation, hasObs bool, now time.Time, staleAfter time.Duration,
	latched, acknowledged string, prevHealthy *metav1.Condition) gpuFaultDecision {
	d := gpuFaultDecision{latch: latched}
	keepLatch := func(reason, msg string) {
		// While a fault is latched the card stays faulted, whatever the signal does; the reason and message are
		// the fault's, not the signal's, so that what an operator reads is what the taint is for.
		if latched != "" {
			r, m := gpuHealthyReasonLatched, "fault "+latched+" is latched"
			if prevHealthy != nil && prevHealthy.Status == metav1.ConditionFalse {
				r, m = prevHealthy.Reason, prevHealthy.Message
			}
			d.healthy = metav1.Condition{Type: conditionGPUHealthy, Status: metav1.ConditionFalse, Reason: r, Message: m}
			d.taint = +1
			return
		}
		d.healthy = metav1.Condition{Type: conditionGPUHealthy, Status: metav1.ConditionUnknown, Reason: reason, Message: msg}
	}

	fresh := hasObs && !obs.LastSuccess.IsZero() && now.Sub(obs.LastSuccess) <= staleAfter
	if !fresh {
		reason, msg := signalReasonNoObservation, "no scrape of this node's exporter has succeeded since the operator started"
		switch {
		case hasObs && obs.LastFailure.After(obs.LastSuccess) && obs.FailureReason != "":
			reason, msg = obs.FailureReason, obs.FailureDetail
		case hasObs && !obs.LastSuccess.IsZero():
			// The message names the bound, not the age: an age would change on every reconcile and turn each one
			// into a status write.
			reason, msg = signalReasonStale, fmt.Sprintf("no successful scrape for more than %s", staleAfter)
		}
		d.signal = metav1.Condition{Type: conditionGPUSignal, Status: metav1.ConditionFalse, Reason: reason, Message: msg}
		keepLatch(gpuHealthyReasonSignalNotFresh, "no fresh reading of this node's cards: "+reason)
		return d
	}
	d.signal = metav1.Condition{Type: conditionGPUSignal, Status: metav1.ConditionTrue, Reason: signalReasonFresh,
		Message: fmt.Sprintf("%d card(s) read within %s", obs.Assessment.Cards, staleAfter)}
	d.reading, d.readingAt = obs.Assessment.Reading(), obs.ReadingSince

	fp := obs.Assessment.Fingerprint()
	switch {
	case fp != "" && fp != acknowledged:
		d.healthy = metav1.Condition{Type: conditionGPUHealthy, Status: metav1.ConditionFalse,
			Reason: obs.Assessment.Reason(), Message: "fault " + fp}
		d.taint = +1
		if fp != latched {
			d.latch, d.newLatch, d.observedAt = fp, true, obs.FaultSince
		}
	case fp != "":
		// Exactly the acknowledged fault: the operator has taken it, and a different one would not match.
		d.healthy = metav1.Condition{Type: conditionGPUHealthy, Status: metav1.ConditionTrue,
			Reason: gpuHealthyReasonAcknowledged, Message: "fault " + fp + " is acknowledged"}
		d.latch, d.taint = "", -1
	case len(obs.Assessment.Missing) > 0:
		// No fault among the fields that were read, and a fault field not read: health cannot be judged.
		keepLatch(gpuHealthyReasonFieldMissing, fmt.Sprintf("no readable value for %v", obs.Assessment.Missing))
	case latched != "" && !obs.Assessment.Pristine():
		// No fault now, and not zero either: an application Xid has replaced the fatal one in the last-Xid field.
		// That is not a repair, so the latch holds until the fields read zero or the fault is acknowledged.
		keepLatch("", "")
	default:
		d.healthy = metav1.Condition{Type: conditionGPUHealthy, Status: metav1.ConditionTrue,
			Reason: gpuHealthyReasonNoFault, Message: "no fault field set on any card"}
		d.latch, d.taint = "", -1
	}
	return d
}

// decideGPUFaultFor reads the inputs of decideGPUFault off the NodeHealth and the store.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) decideGPUFaultFor(nh *platformv1.NodeHealth) gpuFaultDecision {
	obs, ok := r.GPUFault.Store.Get(nh.Spec.NodeName)
	return decideGPUFault(obs, ok, r.GPUFault.now(), r.GPUFault.StaleAfter,
		nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultFingerprint], nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultAcknowledged],
		meta.FindStatusCondition(nh.Status.Conditions, conditionGPUHealthy))
}

// applyGPUFaultToNode changes the node's taints as decided and reports whether they changed.
func applyGPUFaultToNode(node *corev1.Node, d gpuFaultDecision) bool {
	switch d.taint {
	case +1:
		return ensureGPUFaultTaint(node)
	case -1:
		return removeGPUFaultTaint(node)
	}
	return false
}

func isGPUFaultTaint(t corev1.Taint) bool {
	return t.Key == gpuFaultTaintKey && t.Effect == corev1.TaintEffectNoSchedule
}

func ensureGPUFaultTaint(node *corev1.Node) bool {
	if slices.ContainsFunc(node.Spec.Taints, isGPUFaultTaint) {
		return false
	}
	node.Spec.Taints = append(node.Spec.Taints, corev1.Taint{
		Key: gpuFaultTaintKey, Value: "true", Effect: corev1.TaintEffectNoSchedule,
	})
	return true
}

func removeGPUFaultTaint(node *corev1.Node) bool {
	before := len(node.Spec.Taints)
	node.Spec.Taints = slices.DeleteFunc(node.Spec.Taints, isGPUFaultTaint)
	return len(node.Spec.Taints) != before
}

// applyGPUFaultToStatus writes the two conditions; the phase a latched fault means is set by the caller.
func applyGPUFaultToStatus(status *platformv1.NodeHealthStatus, d gpuFaultDecision, generation int64) {
	d.signal.ObservedGeneration = generation
	d.healthy.ObservedGeneration = generation
	meta.SetStatusCondition(&status.Conditions, d.signal)
	meta.SetStatusCondition(&status.Conditions, d.healthy)
}

// recordGPUFault writes the platform's timestamps, displaces GPU work from a faulted node and brings back
// what it displaced. It runs after the Node patch, so that tainted-at is a time the taint existed.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) recordGPUFault(ctx context.Context, nh *platformv1.NodeHealth, d gpuFaultDecision,
	node *corev1.Node) error {
	cfg := r.GPUFault
	base := nh.DeepCopy()
	if nh.Annotations == nil {
		nh.Annotations = map[string]string{}
	}
	prevLatch := nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultFingerprint]
	stamp := func(t time.Time) string { return t.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339Nano) }
	switch {
	case d.newLatch:
		nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultFingerprint] = d.latch
		nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultObservedAt] = stamp(d.observedAt)
		delete(nh.Annotations, annGPUFaultTaintedAt)
		delete(nh.Annotations, annGPUFaultClearedAt)
		gpuFaultTransitionsTotal.WithLabelValues(d.healthy.Reason).Inc()
		cfg.event(nh, corev1.EventTypeWarning, "GPUFaultObserved", "Observe", "node %s: %s", nh.Spec.NodeName, d.latch)
	case prevLatch != "" && d.latch == "":
		delete(nh.Annotations, annGPUFaultFingerprint)
		nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultClearedAt] = stamp(cfg.now())
		gpuFaultTransitionsTotal.WithLabelValues("Cleared").Inc()
		cfg.event(nh, corev1.EventTypeNormal, "GPUFaultCleared", "Clear", "node %s: %s (%s)", nh.Spec.NodeName,
			prevLatch, d.healthy.Reason)
	}
	if d.reading != "" && nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultReading] != d.reading {
		nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultReading] = d.reading
		nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultReadingAt] = stamp(d.readingAt)
	}
	if d.latch != "" && nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultTaintedAt] == "" && slices.ContainsFunc(node.Spec.Taints, isGPUFaultTaint) {
		at := cfg.now()
		nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultTaintedAt] = stamp(at)
		if obs, err := time.Parse(time.RFC3339Nano, nh.Annotations[annGPUFaultObservedAt]); err == nil {
			gpuFaultObserveToTaintSeconds.Observe(at.Sub(obs).Seconds())
		}
	}
	if !equalStringMaps(base.Annotations, nh.Annotations) {
		if err := r.Patch(ctx, nh, client.MergeFrom(base)); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("record gpu fault on nodehealth %s: %w", nh.Name, err)
		}
	}
	if cfg.EvictWorkloads && d.latch != "" {
		if err := r.displaceGPUWork(ctx, nh, d.latch); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return r.reactivateDisplaced(ctx, nh)
}

func equalStringMaps(a, b map[string]string) bool {
	if len(a) != len(b) {
		return false
	}
	for k, v := range a {
		if w, ok := b[k]; !ok || w != v {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// displaceGPUWork deactivates the Kueue Workload of every GPU Pod still on the faulted node.
//
// Deactivation is Kueue's own eviction: it suspends the Job, which stops its Pods and releases the quota, and
// reactivating the Workload requeues it. Deleting the Pods instead would have the Job recreate them inside the
// same admission, so Kueue would never learn that the placement failed.
// A GPU Pod with no Workload is reported and left alone: nothing here may stop work Kueue did not admit.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) displaceGPUWork(ctx context.Context, nh *platformv1.NodeHealth, fp string) error {
	cfg := r.GPUFault
	log := logf.FromContext(ctx)
	var pods corev1.PodList
	if err := cfg.APIReader.List(ctx, &pods, client.MatchingFields{"spec.nodeName": nh.Spec.NodeName}); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("list pods on faulted node %s: %w", nh.Spec.NodeName, err)
	}
	for i := range pods.Items {
		p := &pods.Items[i]
		if p.Status.Phase == corev1.PodSucceeded || p.Status.Phase == corev1.PodFailed || !requestsGPU(p) {
			continue
		}
		owner := metav1.GetControllerOf(p)
		if owner == nil || owner.Kind != "Job" {
			cfg.event(nh, corev1.EventTypeWarning, "GPUFaultPodNotDisplaced", "Displace",
				"pod %s/%s uses a GPU on the faulted node and has no Job owner, so no Kueue Workload to evict", p.Namespace, p.Name)
			continue
		}
		var wls kueuev1beta1.WorkloadList
		if err := r.List(ctx, &wls, client.InNamespace(p.Namespace), client.MatchingLabels{kueueJobUIDLabel: string(owner.UID)}); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("list workloads for pod %s/%s: %w", p.Namespace, p.Name, err)
		}
		if len(wls.Items) == 0 {
			cfg.event(nh, corev1.EventTypeWarning, "GPUFaultPodNotDisplaced", "Displace",
				"pod %s/%s uses a GPU on the faulted node and its Job has no Kueue Workload", p.Namespace, p.Name)
			continue
		}
		for j := range wls.Items {
			wl := &wls.Items[j]
			if !ptr.Deref(wl.Spec.Active, true) {
				continue
			}
			base := wl.DeepCopy()
			wl.Spec.Active = new(false)
			if wl.Annotations == nil {
				wl.Annotations = map[string]string{}
			}
			at := cfg.now()
			wl.Annotations[annWorkloadDeactivatedAt] = at.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339Nano)
			wl.Annotations[annWorkloadDeactivatedNode] = nh.Spec.NodeName
			wl.Annotations[annWorkloadFingerprint] = fp
			delete(wl.Annotations, annWorkloadReactivatedAt)
			if err := r.Patch(ctx, wl, client.MergeFrom(base)); err != nil {
				return fmt.Errorf("deactivate workload %s/%s: %w", wl.Namespace, wl.Name, err)
			}
			gpuFaultWorkloadActionsTotal.WithLabelValues("deactivated").Inc()
			log.Info("Deactivated Workload on a faulted node", "workload", wl.Namespace+"/"+wl.Name, "node", nh.Spec.NodeName)
			cfg.event(wl, corev1.EventTypeWarning, "GPUFaultDeactivated", "Displace",
				"its pod %s ran on node %s, which carries GPU fault %s", p.Name, nh.Spec.NodeName, fp)
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// requestsGPU reports whether any container asks for a GPU, by request or by limit.
func requestsGPU(p *corev1.Pod) bool {
	for _, cs := range [][]corev1.Container{p.Spec.InitContainers, p.Spec.Containers} {
		for _, c := range cs {
			if q, ok := c.Resources.Requests[gpuResourceName]; ok && !q.IsZero() {
				return true
			}
			if q, ok := c.Resources.Limits[gpuResourceName]; ok && !q.IsZero() {
				return true
			}
		}
	}
	return false
}

// reactivateDisplaced reactivates each Workload this platform deactivated for this node, once Kueue has
// finished evicting it.
//
// Waiting for Evicted and for the quota to be gone is what makes the requeue a new admission: reactivating
// while the old one is still being torn down would race Kueue's own eviction.
// Only Workloads carrying deactivated-node are touched, so a Workload a person deactivated stays deactivated.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) reactivateDisplaced(ctx context.Context, nh *platformv1.NodeHealth) error {
	cfg := r.GPUFault
	var wls kueuev1beta1.WorkloadList
	if err := r.List(ctx, &wls); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("list workloads to reactivate: %w", err)
	}
	for i := range wls.Items {
		wl := &wls.Items[i]
		if wl.Annotations[annWorkloadDeactivatedNode] != nh.Spec.NodeName || wl.Annotations[annWorkloadReactivatedAt] != "" ||
			ptr.Deref(wl.Spec.Active, true) ||
			!meta.IsStatusConditionTrue(wl.Status.Conditions, kueuev1beta1.WorkloadEvicted) ||
			meta.IsStatusConditionTrue(wl.Status.Conditions, kueuev1beta1.WorkloadQuotaReserved) {
			continue
		}
		base := wl.DeepCopy()
		wl.Spec.Active = new(true)
		wl.Annotations[annWorkloadReactivatedAt] = cfg.now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339Nano)
		if err := r.Patch(ctx, wl, client.MergeFrom(base)); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("reactivate workload %s/%s: %w", wl.Namespace, wl.Name, err)
		}
		gpuFaultWorkloadActionsTotal.WithLabelValues("reactivated").Inc()
		cfg.event(wl, corev1.EventTypeNormal, "GPUFaultReactivated", "Requeue",
			"evicted from faulted node %s; requeued so Kueue can admit it again", nh.Spec.NodeName)
	}
	return nil
}

// mapWorkloadToNodeHealth wakes the NodeHealth of the node a displaced Workload was taken from, so that its
// eviction finishing is what triggers the reactivation.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) mapWorkloadToNodeHealth(ctx context.Context, obj client.Object) []reconcile.Request {
	node := obj.GetAnnotations()[annWorkloadDeactivatedNode]
	if node == "" {
		return nil
	}
	return r.mapNodeToNodeHealth(ctx, &metav1.PartialObjectMetadata{Name: node})
}

// Package gpufault reads the GPU fault fields a DCGM exporter serves, decides which of them mean the card is
// faulted, and scores the fault-reaction study registered in
// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-gpu-fault-reaction-design.md.
//
// It is a package of its own, and not part of internal/queuelab, because the two read the same exporter for
// opposite purposes: queuelab asks whether a card did work, this asks whether a card can be trusted with any.
package gpufault

import (
	"bufio"
	"bytes"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"math"
	"sort"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// The three series this package reads, by the names dcgm-exporter gives DCGM fields 230, 311 and 310
// (dcgmlib/dcgm_fields.h; the exporter's default-counters.csv).
const (
	// FieldXid is the value of the last Xid error DCGM recorded for the card, 0 when there has been none.
	FieldXid = "DCGM_FI_DEV_XID_ERRORS"
	// FieldECCDoubleBit is the volatile count of uncorrected double-bit ECC errors since the driver loaded.
	FieldECCDoubleBit = "DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_DBE_VOL_TOTAL"
	// FieldECCSingleBit is the volatile count of corrected single-bit ECC errors, which is never a fault here.
	FieldECCSingleBit = "DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_SBE_VOL_TOTAL"
)

// Reasons a fault carries, which the controller copies into the GPUHealthy condition.
const (
	ReasonXid           = "XidError"
	ReasonECCDoubleBit  = "EccDoubleBitError"
	ReasonMultipleFault = "MultipleFaults"
)

// faultFields are the fields whose absence means the card's health cannot be judged.
//
// The single-bit count is not among them: it is read so the benign arm can show a change that is ignored,
// and a card that does not export it has lost nothing the policy needs.
var faultFields = []string{FieldXid, FieldECCDoubleBit}

// applicationXids are the Xids NVIDIA's device plugin ignores as application errors.
//
// Borrowed rather than chosen, from k8s-device-plugin v0.16.2 internal/rm/health.go ("Application errors: the
// GPU should still be healthy"), so that this platform's quarantine and the plugin's own unhealthy rule agree
// on which Xids are about the card. Every other non-zero Xid is a fault.
var applicationXids = map[int64]bool{13: true, 31: true, 43: true, 45: true, 68: true}

// GPUReading is what one scrape said about one card.
type GPUReading struct {
	// UUID identifies the card across scrapes; the gpu index can be renumbered, the UUID cannot.
	UUID string
	// GPU is the exporter's index label, kept for messages.
	GPU string
	// Values holds every fault-relevant field the scrape carried a readable value for.
	Values map[string]float64
	// Unavailable names the fault-relevant fields the scrape carried with a value this parser cannot read.
	Unavailable map[string]bool
}

// errUnreadable marks one sample line whose value is not a number, as distinct from a malformed scrape.
var errUnreadable = errors.New("value is not a number")

// ParseExposition reads the three fields out of one exporter scrape, keyed by card UUID.
//
// A line that is malformed is an error for the whole scrape, because a parser that skipped what it could not
// read would turn a broken exporter into a healthy-looking one.
// A value DCGM renders as unavailable is recorded as such, not skipped, for the same reason.
func ParseExposition(body []byte) (map[string]*GPUReading, error) {
	out := map[string]*GPUReading{}
	sc := bufio.NewScanner(bytes.NewReader(body))
	sc.Buffer(make([]byte, 0, 64*1024), 1024*1024)
	for sc.Scan() {
		line := strings.TrimSpace(sc.Text())
		if line == "" || strings.HasPrefix(line, "#") {
			continue
		}
		name := metricName(line)
		if name != FieldXid && name != FieldECCDoubleBit && name != FieldECCSingleBit {
			continue
		}
		labels, value, err := parseSample(line)
		if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, errUnreadable) {
			return nil, err
		}
		uuid := labels["UUID"]
		if uuid == "" {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("a %s sample names no card UUID: %q", name, line)
		}
		r := out[uuid]
		if r == nil {
			r = &GPUReading{UUID: uuid, GPU: labels["gpu"], Values: map[string]float64{}, Unavailable: map[string]bool{}}
			out[uuid] = r
		}
		// The exporter emits one series per card and field, but with Kubernetes mapping on it emits one per
		// Pod as well; the values are the card's either way, and two that disagree mean the scrape cannot be
		// trusted for this card.
		if errors.Is(err, errUnreadable) {
			r.Unavailable[name] = true
			continue
		}
		if prev, ok := r.Values[name]; ok && prev != value {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("card %s carries two different %s values in one scrape: %v and %v", uuid, name, prev, value)
		}
		r.Values[name] = value
	}
	if err := sc.Err(); err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("read scrape: %w", err)
	}
	return out, nil
}

// metricName returns the series name of an exposition line.
func metricName(line string) string {
	if i := strings.IndexAny(line, "{ "); i >= 0 {
		return line[:i]
	}
	return line
}

// parseSample splits one exposition line into its labels and value.
//
// It refuses rather than guesses: no label set, a label with no value, or nothing after the label set.
// A trailing Prometheus timestamp is allowed and ignored, because the value is what is read.
func parseSample(line string) (map[string]string, float64, error) {
	open := strings.IndexByte(line, '{')
	closeAt := strings.LastIndexByte(line, '}')
	if open < 0 || closeAt < open {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("no label set in %q", line)
	}
	labels := map[string]string{}
	for _, pair := range splitLabels(line[open+1 : closeAt]) {
		if strings.TrimSpace(pair) == "" {
			continue
		}
		k, v, ok := strings.Cut(pair, "=")
		if !ok {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("label %q in %q has no value", pair, line)
		}
		labels[strings.TrimSpace(k)] = strings.Trim(strings.TrimSpace(v), `"`)
	}
	rest := strings.Fields(line[closeAt+1:])
	if len(rest) == 0 {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("no value in %q", line)
	}
	v, err := strconv.ParseFloat(rest[0], 64)
	if err != nil || math.IsNaN(v) || math.IsInf(v, 0) {
		return labels, 0, errUnreadable
	}
	return labels, v, nil
}

// splitLabels splits a label set on commas outside quoted values.
//
// DCGM's modelName can carry commas, and a naive split would make a good scrape a parse error.
func splitLabels(s string) []string {
	var out []string
	var cur strings.Builder
	inQuote, escaped := false, false
	for i := 0; i < len(s); i++ {
		c := s[i]
		switch {
		case escaped:
			escaped = false
			cur.WriteByte(c)
		case c == '\\' && inQuote:
			escaped = true
			cur.WriteByte(c)
		case c == '"':
			inQuote = !inQuote
			cur.WriteByte(c)
		case c == ',' && !inQuote:
			out = append(out, cur.String())
			cur.Reset()
		default:
			cur.WriteByte(c)
		}
	}
	if cur.Len() > 0 {
		out = append(out, cur.String())
	}
	return out
}

// Fault is one field on one card that the policy calls a fault.
type Fault struct {
	UUID   string
	GPU    string
	Field  string
	Value  int64
	Reason string
}

// Assessment is the policy's reading of one scrape.
type Assessment struct {
	// Faults is sorted by card UUID and then field, so the fingerprint is the same for the same state.
	Faults []Fault
	// Missing names "UUID/field" for each fault field a card did not carry a readable value for.
	Missing []string
	// Cards is how many cards the scrape carried any of the three fields for.
	Cards int

	// nonZero is true when a fault field read non-zero without being a fault: an application Xid.
	nonZero bool
	// reading is "UUID/field=value" for every readable field, sorted.
	reading string
}

// Pristine reports whether every card carried every fault field and each read zero: no Xid recorded and no
// double-bit error counted since the host engine or the driver last started.
//
// It is what releases a latched fault, and "no fault" is not enough for that. The Xid field holds the last Xid
// only, so an application Xid arriving after a fatal one replaces it and reads as no fault while nothing about
// the card was repaired; zero is the value a GPU reset or a driver reload leaves.
func (a Assessment) Pristine() bool {
	return a.Cards > 0 && len(a.Missing) == 0 && len(a.Faults) == 0 && !a.nonZero
}

// Reading names every value the scrape carried, the single-bit count included, so that a change the policy
// ignores is still a change the platform can be shown to have read.
func (a Assessment) Reading() string { return a.reading }

// Fingerprint names the fault state exactly: two scrapes have the same fingerprint when the same cards carry
// the same fault values.
//
// It is what an operator acknowledges, so a new fault -- another card, another Xid, a higher count -- changes
// it and is not covered by an acknowledgement of the old one.
func (a Assessment) Fingerprint() string {
	parts := make([]string, 0, len(a.Faults))
	for _, f := range a.Faults {
		parts = append(parts, fmt.Sprintf("%s/%s=%d", f.UUID, f.Field, f.Value))
	}
	return strings.Join(parts, ",")
}

// Reason is the condition reason for the faults: the one kind when there is one, MultipleFaults otherwise.
func (a Assessment) Reason() string {
	reason := ""
	for _, f := range a.Faults {
		switch {
		case reason == "":
			reason = f.Reason
		case reason != f.Reason:
			return ReasonMultipleFault
		}
	}
	return reason
}

// Assess applies the fault policy to one scrape's readings.
//
// An Xid is a fault unless it is one of the application Xids; a double-bit count above zero is a fault; the
// single-bit count is never one. A value that is not a whole non-negative number is refused, because a field
// DCGM defines as an Xid code or a count cannot honestly be anything else and rounding it would invent one.
func Assess(readings map[string]*GPUReading) (Assessment, error) {
	var a Assessment
	uuids := make([]string, 0, len(readings))
	for u := range readings {
		uuids = append(uuids, u)
	}
	sort.Strings(uuids)
	a.Cards = len(uuids)
	var reading []string
	for _, u := range uuids {
		r := readings[u]
		for _, field := range []string{FieldXid, FieldECCDoubleBit, FieldECCSingleBit} {
			if v, ok := r.Values[field]; ok {
				reading = append(reading, fmt.Sprintf("%s/%s=%s", u, field, strconv.FormatFloat(v, 'f', -1, 64)))
			}
		}
		for _, field := range faultFields {
			v, ok := r.Values[field]
			if !ok {
				a.Missing = append(a.Missing, u+"/"+field)
				continue
			}
			if v < 0 || v != math.Trunc(v) {
				return Assessment{}, fmt.Errorf("card %s: %s is %v, not a whole non-negative number", u, field, v)
			}
			n := int64(v)
			switch {
			case field == FieldXid && n != 0 && !applicationXids[n]:
				a.Faults = append(a.Faults, Fault{UUID: u, GPU: r.GPU, Field: field, Value: n, Reason: ReasonXid})
			case field == FieldECCDoubleBit && n > 0:
				a.Faults = append(a.Faults, Fault{UUID: u, GPU: r.GPU, Field: field, Value: n, Reason: ReasonECCDoubleBit})
			case n != 0:
				a.nonZero = true
			}
		}
	}
	a.reading = strings.Join(reading, ",")
	return a, nil
}

package controller

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"

	corev1 "k8s.io/api/core/v1"
	"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/api/equality"
	apierrors "k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/api/errors"
	"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/runtime"
	"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/types"
	ctrl "sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/client"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/controller/controllerutil"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/handler"
	logf "sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/log"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/reconcile"
	"sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/source"
	kueuev1beta1 "sigs.k8s.io/kueue/apis/kueue/v1beta1"

	platformv1 "github.com/lkhun9311/gpu-mlops-platform-control-plane/api/v1"
)

// NodeHealthReconciler reconciles a NodeHealth object
type NodeHealthReconciler struct {
	client.Client
	Scheme *runtime.Scheme
	// GPUFault turns on the DCGM fault path; nil keeps the reconciler readiness-only, as it always was.
	GPUFault *GPUFaultConfig
}

// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=platform.lkhun9311.github.io,resources=nodehealths,verbs=get;list;watch;create;update;patch;delete
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=platform.lkhun9311.github.io,resources=nodehealths/status,verbs=get;update;patch
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=platform.lkhun9311.github.io,resources=nodehealths/finalizers,verbs=update
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups="",resources=nodes,verbs=get;list;watch;update;patch
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups="",resources=pods,verbs=get;list
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=kueue.x-k8s.io,resources=workloads,verbs=get;list;watch;patch
// +kubebuilder:rbac:groups=events.k8s.io,resources=events,verbs=create;patch

// Reconcile observes the target Node, reflects its readiness into NodeHealth status, and enforces quarantine:
// a not-ready node is tainted so the scheduler stops placing GPU workloads on it,
// and the taint is removed when the node recovers or the NodeHealth is deleted.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, req ctrl.Request) (ctrl.Result, error) {
	log := logf.FromContext(ctx)

	var nh platformv1.NodeHealth
	if err := r.Get(ctx, req.NamespacedName, &nh); err != nil {
		return ctrl.Result{}, client.IgnoreNotFound(err)
	}

	// Handle deletion: remove our taint from the node, then drop the finalizer.
	if !nh.DeletionTimestamp.IsZero() {
		if controllerutil.ContainsFinalizer(&nh, nodeHealthFinalizer) {
			var node corev1.Node
			switch err := r.Get(ctx, types.NamespacedName{Name: nh.Spec.NodeName}, &node); {
			case err == nil:
				base := node.DeepCopy()
				// The gpu-fault taint is this resource's too, so it goes with the resource whether or not the
				// fault path is still enabled; a taint nobody will ever remove is the worse failure.
				removedGPU := removeGPUFaultTaint(&node)
				if removeUnhealthyTaint(&node) || removedGPU {
					if err := r.Patch(ctx, &node, client.MergeFrom(base)); err != nil {
						return ctrl.Result{}, fmt.Errorf("remove unhealthy taint from node %s on deletion: %w", node.Name, err)
					}
					log.Info("Removed unhealthy taint on deletion", "node", node.Name)
				}
			case apierrors.IsNotFound(err):
				// Node already gone; nothing to clean up.
			default:
				return ctrl.Result{}, fmt.Errorf("get node %s on deletion: %w", nh.Spec.NodeName, err)
			}
			controllerutil.RemoveFinalizer(&nh, nodeHealthFinalizer)
			if err := r.Update(ctx, &nh); err != nil {
				return ctrl.Result{}, fmt.Errorf("remove finalizer from nodehealth %s: %w", nh.Name, err)
			}
		}
		return ctrl.Result{}, nil
	}

	// Ensure the finalizer is present before doing work.
	if !controllerutil.ContainsFinalizer(&nh, nodeHealthFinalizer) {
		controllerutil.AddFinalizer(&nh, nodeHealthFinalizer)
		if err := r.Update(ctx, &nh); err != nil {
			return ctrl.Result{}, fmt.Errorf("add finalizer to nodehealth %s: %w", nh.Name, err)
		}
		return ctrl.Result{}, nil
	}

	// Observe the target Node and compute the desired status + taint enforcement.
	desired := nh.Status.DeepCopy()
	desired.ObservedGeneration = nh.Generation

	var node corev1.Node
	var nodeBase *corev1.Node
	// nodeChanged is the readiness taint's change alone, because the taint metric below counts only that one.
	nodeChanged := false
	err := r.Get(ctx, types.NamespacedName{Name: nh.Spec.NodeName}, &node)
	// The DCGM path runs only on a node that exists, and only when it was turned on.
	// It is decided before the phase is set, so that a latched GPU fault quarantines a node whose kubelet is
	// Ready without the phase passing through Ready, which would move lastTransitionTime on every reconcile.
	var gpuDecision *gpuFaultDecision
	if r.GPUFault != nil && err == nil {
		d := r.decideGPUFaultFor(&nh)
		gpuDecision = &d
	}
	// readinessTaint is "applied" or "removed" when the readiness taint changed, for the metric that counts only it.
	readinessTaint := ""
	switch {
	case apierrors.IsNotFound(err):
		// No node to manage: report Pending and clear any fault signal.
		setPhase(desired, phasePending)
		desired.FaultSignal = nil
		setReadyCondition(desired, false, reasonNodeNotFound, "Target node not found", nh.Generation)
	case err != nil:
		return ctrl.Result{}, fmt.Errorf("get node %s: %w", nh.Spec.NodeName, err)
	case isNodeReady(&node):
		nodeBase = node.DeepCopy()
		if gpuDecision != nil && gpuDecision.latch != "" {
			setPhase(desired, phaseQuarantine)
			desired.FaultSignal = &platformv1.FaultSignal{Source: faultSourceDCGM, Code: gpuDecision.latch}
		} else {
			setPhase(desired, phaseReady)
			desired.FaultSignal = nil
		}
		if removeUnhealthyTaint(&node) {
			nodeChanged, readinessTaint = true, "removed"
		}
		setReadyCondition(desired, true, reasonNodeReady, "Target node is Ready", nh.Generation)
	default:
		nodeBase = node.DeepCopy()
		setPhase(desired, phaseQuarantine)
		desired.FaultSignal = &platformv1.FaultSignal{Source: faultSourceNodeNotReady}
		if ensureUnhealthyTaint(&node) {
			nodeChanged, readinessTaint = true, "applied"
		}
		setReadyCondition(desired, false, reasonNodeNotReady, "Target node is not Ready", nh.Generation)
	}
	if gpuDecision != nil {
		if applyGPUFaultToNode(&node, *gpuDecision) {
			nodeChanged = true
		}
		applyGPUFaultToStatus(desired, *gpuDecision, nh.Generation)
	}

	// Enforce taint changes on the node first, so status never claims a quarantine we failed to apply.
	// Patch only the taint delta from the pre-mutation base, so concurrent kubelet updates to the hot Node object are not clobbered.
	if nodeChanged {
		if err := r.Patch(ctx, &node, client.MergeFrom(nodeBase)); err != nil {
			return ctrl.Result{}, fmt.Errorf("update node %s taints: %w", node.Name, err)
		}
		log.Info("Updated node taints", "node", node.Name, "phase", desired.Phase)

		// Count the taint transition only after the patch succeeds, so the metric never claims an enforcement that did not happen.
		//
		// Only the readiness taint is counted here; the gpu-fault taint has its own series.
		if readinessTaint != "" {
			nodeHealthTaintTotal.WithLabelValues(readinessTaint).Inc()
		}
	}
	// After the Node patch, so that tainted-at is a time the taint existed.
	if gpuDecision != nil {
		if err := r.recordGPUFault(ctx, &nh, *gpuDecision, &node); err != nil {
			return ctrl.Result{}, err
		}
	}

	// Idempotent: write status only when it actually changed.
	if !equality.Semantic.DeepEqual(nh.Status, *desired) {
		nh.Status = *desired
		if err := r.Status().Update(ctx, &nh); err != nil {
			return ctrl.Result{}, fmt.Errorf("update nodehealth status %s: %w", nh.Name, err)
		}
		log.Info("Updated NodeHealth status", "name", nh.Name, "phase", desired.Phase)
	}

	return ctrl.Result{}, nil
}

// NodeNameIndex is the cache field-index key over NodeHealth.spec.nodeName.
//
// It mirrors the gateway's ModelNameIndex: the lookup that needs it runs on the hot path, so it resolves against the informer cache with no field selector and no apiserver call.
const NodeNameIndex = ".spec.nodeName"

// indexNodeHealthByNodeName is the extractor behind NodeNameIndex.
//
// It is a named function rather than a literal because the tests must register the identical extractor to make MatchingFields behave as it does against the manager's cache.
func indexNodeHealthByNodeName(o client.Object) []string {
	return []string{o.(*platformv1.NodeHealth).Spec.NodeName}
}

// mapNodeToNodeHealth maps a Node event to reconcile requests for every NodeHealth whose spec.nodeName matches the node.
// This propagates node-side drift back into status.
//
// The lookup goes through NodeNameIndex rather than listing and filtering in Go, because kubelet posts a status heartbeat for every node on a fixed cadence: an unindexed List would walk every NodeHealth in the cluster once per node per heartbeat, so its cost grows with the product of the two rather than with the handful of objects that actually match.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) mapNodeToNodeHealth(ctx context.Context, obj client.Object) []reconcile.Request {
	var list platformv1.NodeHealthList
	if err := r.List(ctx, &list, client.MatchingFields{NodeNameIndex: obj.GetName()}); err != nil {
		// A map function has no error return, so the only alternative to logging is dropping the failure on the floor.
		//
		// That is the worst outcome available here: drift stops reaching status and the resource simply stops updating, with nothing anywhere saying which node stopped being watched or why.
		logf.FromContext(ctx).Error(err, "Could not list NodeHealth resources for Node event", "node", obj.GetName())
		return nil
	}
	reqs := make([]reconcile.Request, 0, len(list.Items))
	for i := range list.Items {
		reqs = append(reqs, reconcile.Request{
			Name: list.Items[i].Name,
		})
	}
	return reqs
}

// SetupWithManager sets up the controller with the Manager.
func (r *NodeHealthReconciler) SetupWithManager(mgr ctrl.Manager) error {
	// The index must exist before the Node watch can fire, so it is registered here rather than lazily.
	//
	// context.Background() rather than a scoped context because IndexField only installs the extractor on the cache; it starts nothing that would need cancelling.
	if err := mgr.GetFieldIndexer().IndexField(context.Background(), &platformv1.NodeHealth{}, NodeNameIndex, indexNodeHealthByNodeName); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("index nodehealth by %s: %w", NodeNameIndex, err)
	}
	b := ctrl.NewControllerManagedBy(mgr).
		For(&platformv1.NodeHealth{}).
		Watches(&corev1.Node{}, handler.EnqueueRequestsFromMapFunc(r.mapNodeToNodeHealth))
	if r.GPUFault != nil {
		if r.GPUFault.Events == nil || r.GPUFault.Store == nil || r.GPUFault.APIReader == nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("gpu fault path enabled without its store, wake-up channel or API reader")
		}
		// The scraper's wake-ups, and the displaced Workloads, whose eviction finishing is what lets them be
		// reactivated; without the second watch a reactivation would wait for the next Node heartbeat.
		b = b.WatchesRawSource(source.Channel(r.GPUFault.Events, &handler.EnqueueRequestForObject{})).
			Watches(&kueuev1beta1.Workload{}, handler.EnqueueRequestsFromMapFunc(r.mapWorkloadToNodeHealth))
	}
	return b.Named("nodehealth").Complete(r)
}

package main

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"sort"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"time"

// The fake DCGM is the kind rehearsal's stand-in for the two halves of DCGM the fault-reaction study touches
// (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-gpu-fault-reaction-design.md): a field cache that `dcgmi test --inject`
// writes and `--introspect` reads, and a dcgm-exporter that serves a snapshot of that cache taken once per
// collection period.
//
// It is off unless FAKE_DCGM_ADDR is set, so the device plugin every other study runs is unchanged.
// What it does not model is said here rather than discovered later: there is no NVML, so nothing ever writes a
// field except an injection, and an injected value therefore persists whatever its offset; on a real card the
// smoke cell is what shows whether it does.

// fakeDCGMFields are the fields the fake knows, by DCGM field ID (dcgmlib/dcgm_fields.h) and exporter name.
var fakeDCGMFields = map[int]string{
	230: "DCGM_FI_DEV_XID_ERRORS",
	310: "DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_SBE_VOL_TOTAL",
	311: "DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_DBE_VOL_TOTAL",
}

type fakeDCGMKey struct{ gpu, field int }

type fakeDCGMSample struct {
	value int64
	// at is the sample's DCGM timestamp: the injection time plus its offset, as dcgmi's --offset sets it.
	at time.Time
}

// fakeDCGM holds the cache and the exporter's last snapshot.
type fakeDCGM struct {
	mu       sync.Mutex
	hostname string
	gpus     int
	cache    map[fakeDCGMKey]fakeDCGMSample
	snapshot []byte
	now      func() time.Time
	// down makes /metrics answer 503, as an exporter that has gone away would leave its scraper failing.
	down bool
}

func newFakeDCGM(hostname string, gpus int, now func() time.Time) *fakeDCGM {
	d := &fakeDCGM{hostname: hostname, gpus: gpus, cache: map[fakeDCGMKey]fakeDCGMSample{}, now: now}
	for g := range gpus {
		for f := range fakeDCGMFields {
			d.cache[fakeDCGMKey{g, f}] = fakeDCGMSample{value: 0, at: now()}
		}
	}
	d.collect()
	return d
}

// inject writes a sample the way dcgmInjectFieldValue does, and keeps whichever sample is newest by timestamp.
//
// Newest by timestamp, not last written, is the reading the registration's fixed 900 s offset rests on; the
// fake implements that reading so that the runner's offsets are exercised, not so that it is confirmed.
func (d *fakeDCGM) inject(gpu, field int, value int64, offset time.Duration) error {
	if _, ok := fakeDCGMFields[field]; !ok {
		return fmt.Errorf("field %d is not one the fake knows", field)
	}
	if gpu < 0 || gpu >= d.gpus {
		return fmt.Errorf("gpu %d does not exist; this node has %d", gpu, d.gpus)
	}
	if value < 0 {
		return fmt.Errorf("value %d is negative", value)
	}
	d.mu.Lock()
	defer d.mu.Unlock()
	s := fakeDCGMSample{value: value, at: d.now().Add(offset)}
	k := fakeDCGMKey{gpu, field}
	if cur, ok := d.cache[k]; ok && cur.at.After(s.at) {
		return fmt.Errorf("an existing sample at %s is newer than this one at %s, so the cache would not show it",
			cur.at.Format(time.RFC3339Nano), s.at.Format(time.RFC3339Nano))
	}
	d.cache[k] = s
	return nil
}

func (d *fakeDCGM) introspect(gpu, field int) (int64, bool) {
	d.mu.Lock()
	defer d.mu.Unlock()
	s, ok := d.cache[fakeDCGMKey{gpu, field}]
	return s.value, ok
}

// collect takes the exporter's snapshot, as dcgm-exporter does once per collection interval.
func (d *fakeDCGM) collect() {
	d.mu.Lock()
	defer d.mu.Unlock()
	ids := make([]int, 0, len(fakeDCGMFields))
	for f := range fakeDCGMFields {
		ids = append(ids, f)
	}
	sort.Ints(ids)
	var b strings.Builder
	for _, f := range ids {
		name := fakeDCGMFields[f]
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "# HELP %s fake DCGM field %d.\n# TYPE %s gauge\n", name, f, name)
		for g := range d.gpus {
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "%s{gpu=\"%d\",UUID=\"fake-gpu-%d\",device=\"nvidia%d\",modelName=\"gpu-simulator\",Hostname=%q} %d\n",
				name, g, g, g, d.hostname, d.cache[fakeDCGMKey{g, f}].value)
		}
	}
	d.snapshot = []byte(b.String())
}

func (d *fakeDCGM) isDown() bool {
	d.mu.Lock()
	defer d.mu.Unlock()
	return d.down
}

func (d *fakeDCGM) setDown(down bool) {
	d.mu.Lock()
	defer d.mu.Unlock()
	d.down = down
}

func (d *fakeDCGM) metrics() []byte {
	d.mu.Lock()
	defer d.mu.Unlock()
	return d.snapshot
}

// ServeHTTP is the exporter's /metrics and the two dcgmi verbs the study uses.
//
// POST /inject?gpu=G&field=F&value=V[&offset=S] and GET /introspect?gpu=G&field=F, each answering one line.
func (d *fakeDCGM) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	q := r.URL.Query()
	intArg := func(name string, def int64, required bool) (int64, error) {
		raw := q.Get(name)
		if raw == "" {
			if required {
				return 0, fmt.Errorf("%s is required", name)
			}
			return def, nil
		}
		return strconv.ParseInt(raw, 10, 64)
	}
	switch r.URL.Path {
	case "/metrics":
		if d.isDown() {
			http.Error(w, "the fake exporter is down", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
			return
		}
		_, _ = w.Write(d.metrics())
	case "/exporter":
		// The stale-signal checks take the exporter away while the operator runs; on a real node that is the
		// exporter Pod leaving, here it is this switch, so the device plugin keeps the node's capacity meanwhile.
		state := q.Get("state")
		if r.Method != http.MethodPost || (state != "up" && state != "down") {
			http.Error(w, "POST /exporter?state=up|down", http.StatusBadRequest)
			return
		}
		d.setDown(state == "down")
		_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "exporter %s\n", state)
	case "/health":
		_, _ = w.Write([]byte("ok\n"))
	case "/inject":
		if r.Method != http.MethodPost {
			http.Error(w, "inject writes the cache; POST it", http.StatusMethodNotAllowed)
			return
		}
		gpu, err1 := intArg("gpu", 0, true)
		field, err2 := intArg("field", 0, true)
		value, err3 := intArg("value", 0, true)
		// dcgmi's own default offset is 1 s.
		offset, err4 := intArg("offset", 1, false)
		if err := errors.Join(err1, err2, err3, err4); err != nil {
			http.Error(w, err.Error(), http.StatusBadRequest)
			return
		}
		if err := d.inject(int(gpu), int(field), value, time.Duration(offset)*time.Second); err != nil {
			http.Error(w, err.Error(), http.StatusConflict)
			return
		}
		_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "injected gpu=%d field=%d value=%d offset=%d\n", gpu, field, value, offset)
	case "/introspect":
		gpu, err1 := intArg("gpu", 0, true)
		field, err2 := intArg("field", 0, true)
		if err := errors.Join(err1, err2); err != nil {
			http.Error(w, err.Error(), http.StatusBadRequest)
			return
		}
		v, ok := d.introspect(int(gpu), int(field))
		if !ok {
			http.Error(w, "no such gpu or field", http.StatusNotFound)
			return
		}
		_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "%d\n", v)
	default:
		http.NotFound(w, r)
	}
}

// fakeDCGMConfig reads FAKE_DCGM_ADDR and FAKE_DCGM_COLLECT_MS; an empty address means the fake is off.
func fakeDCGMConfig(getenv func(string) string) (string, time.Duration, error) {
	addr := getenv("FAKE_DCGM_ADDR")
	if addr == "" {
		return "", 0, nil
	}
	if _, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(addr); err != nil {
		return "", 0, fmt.Errorf("FAKE_DCGM_ADDR %q: %w", addr, err)
	}
	// 1,000 ms by default, the -c the study's exporter is run with.
	ms := int64(1000)
	if raw := getenv("FAKE_DCGM_COLLECT_MS"); raw != "" {
		v, err := strconv.ParseInt(raw, 10, 64)
		if err != nil || v <= 0 {
			return "", 0, fmt.Errorf("FAKE_DCGM_COLLECT_MS %q must be a positive whole number of milliseconds", raw)
		}
		ms = v
	}
	return addr, time.Duration(ms) * time.Millisecond, nil
}

// serveFakeDCGM runs the fake until ctx ends.
func serveFakeDCGM(ctx context.Context, d *fakeDCGM, addr string, collect time.Duration) error {
	srv := &http.Server{Addr: addr, Handler: d, ReadHeaderTimeout: 5 * time.Second}
	go func() {
		t := time.NewTicker(collect)
		defer t.Stop()
		for {
			select {
			case <-ctx.Done():
				_ = srv.Close()
				return
			case <-t.C:
				d.collect()
			}
		}
	}()
	if err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, http.ErrServerClosed) {
		return err
	}
	return nil
}
