package gpufault

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"sort"
	"strings"
)

// ScoreSession scores every repetition under dir/reps and reads dir/smoke.env for arms the smoke cell stopped.
//
// A repetition directory that cannot be read at all is an error for the session, not a skipped repetition:
// the verdict counts valid repetitions, and one that silently vanished would raise no flag anywhere.
func ScoreSession(dir string) (Report, error) {
	entries, err := os.ReadDir(filepath.Join(dir, "reps"))
	if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
		return Report{}, err
	}
	var cells []CellResult
	for _, e := range entries {
		if !e.IsDir() {
			continue
		}
		c, err := ScoreCell(filepath.Join(dir, "reps", e.Name()))
		if err != nil {
			return Report{}, fmt.Errorf("repetition %s: %w", e.Name(), err)
		}
		cells = append(cells, c)
	}
	sort.Slice(cells, func(i, j int) bool { return cells[i].Dir < cells[j].Dir })
	notRun := map[string]bool{}
	smoke, err := readEnv(filepath.Join(dir, "smoke.env"))
	switch {
	case errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist):
		// The rehearsal has a smoke step too; a session without one never established its injection.
		r := Judge(cells, notRun)
		r.Refuse = append(r.Refuse, "no smoke.env: the session never recorded whether injection reaches the exporter")
		r.Label = "REFUSED"
		return r, nil
	case err != nil:
		return Report{}, err
	}
	for _, arm := range Arms {
		if v := smoke["arm_"+arm]; v != "run" {
			notRun[arm] = true
		}
	}
	r := Judge(cells, notRun)
	for _, name := range StaleChecks {
		r.Checks = append(r.Checks, judgeStaleCheck(filepath.Join(dir, "checks", name), name))
	}
	return r, nil
}

// StaleChecks are the two registered cells that take the exporter away with the operator running.
var StaleChecks = []string{"stale-healthy", "stale-latched"}

// staleSignalReasons are the GPUSignal reasons that mean "no fresh reading", as the controller writes them.
var staleSignalReasons = map[string]bool{"Stale": true, "NoExporter": true, "ScrapeFailed": true, "AmbiguousExporter": true, "NoCards": true}

// judgeStaleCheck compares what the runner recorded with what the registration says must hold:
// without a fresh reading the platform neither sets nor clears a fault, and says so in GPUSignal.
func judgeStaleCheck(dir, name string) CheckResult {
	c := CheckResult{Name: name, Verdict: "PASS"}
	env, err := readEnv(filepath.Join(dir, "check.env"))
	if err != nil {
		return CheckResult{Name: name, Verdict: "NOT-RUN", Reasons: []string{err.Error()}}
	}
	expect := func(ok bool, f string, a ...any) {
		if !ok {
			c.Verdict = "FAIL"
			c.Reasons = append(c.Reasons, fmt.Sprintf(f, a...))
		}
	}
	expect(env["during_signal_status"] == "False" && staleSignalReasons[env["during_signal_reason"]],
		"GPUSignal while the exporter was away: %s/%s, want False with a no-reading reason", env["during_signal_status"], env["during_signal_reason"])
	switch name {
	case "stale-healthy":
		expect(env["during_healthy_status"] == "Unknown", "GPUHealthy %q while away, want Unknown, never True", env["during_healthy_status"])
		expect(env["during_taint"] == "absent" && env["during_latched"] == "", "stale data set a fault: taint %s, latched %q", env["during_taint"], env["during_latched"])
	case "stale-latched":
		expect(env["before_latched"] != "", "the fault was never latched before the exporter was taken away")
		expect(env["during_healthy_status"] == "False" && env["during_taint"] == "present" && env["during_latched"] == env["before_latched"],
			"stale data released the fault: GPUHealthy %s, taint %s, latched %q", env["during_healthy_status"], env["during_taint"], env["during_latched"])
		expect(env["after_latched"] == "" && env["after_taint"] == "absent",
			"a fresh zero reading did not clear it: latched %q, taint %s", env["after_latched"], env["after_taint"])
	}
	return c
}

// String renders the report for a terminal and for the evidence directory.
func (r Report) String() string {
	var b strings.Builder
	fmt.Fprintf(&b, "GPU FAULT REACTION: %s\n", r.Label)
	for _, s := range r.Refuse {
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "  refused: %s\n", s)
	}
	fmt.Fprintf(&b, "P5 (allocatable unchanged by injection): %s\n", r.P5)
	for _, a := range r.Arms {
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "arm %-6s %-22s valid %d of %d\n", a.Arm, a.Verdict, a.Valid, a.Total)
		hops := make([]string, 0, len(a.Summary))
		for h := range a.Summary {
			hops = append(hops, h)
		}
		sort.Strings(hops)
		for _, h := range hops {
			s := a.Summary[h]
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "    %-14s median %8.3f s  min %8.3f  max %8.3f   (upper ends)\n", h, s[0], s[1], s[2])
		}
		for _, reason := range a.Reasons {
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "    - %s\n", reason)
		}
	}
	for _, c := range r.Checks {
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "check %-14s %s\n", c.Name, c.Verdict)
		for _, reason := range c.Reasons {
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "    - %s\n", reason)
		}
	}
	return b.String()
}

package main

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"flag"
	"fmt"
	"os"

	"github.com/lkhun9311/gpu-mlops-platform-control-plane/internal/gpufault"
)

// gpuFaultVerdict scores a fault-reaction session directory and prints the registered verdict.
//
// It exits non-zero when it refuses, so a script that pipes it cannot read a refusal as a verdict; a verdict of
// any kind, DID-NOT-REACT and FALSE-POSITIVE included, is a judgement and exits 0.
func gpuFaultVerdict(args []string) error {
	fs := flag.NewFlagSet("gpu-fault-verdict", flag.ExitOnError)
	dir := fs.String("dir", "", "the session directory, holding reps/ and smoke.env")
	jsonOut := fs.String("json", "", "also write the full report, every repetition included, as JSON to this file")
	if err := fs.Parse(args); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if *dir == "" {
		return fmt.Errorf("--dir is required")
	}
	r, err := gpufault.ScoreSession(*dir)
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Println("GPU FAULT REACTION: UNREADABLE")
		return err
	}
	fmt.Print(r.String())
	if *jsonOut != "" {
		b, err := json.MarshalIndent(r, "", "  ")
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if err := os.WriteFile(*jsonOut, append(b, '\n'), 0o644); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	if r.Label == "REFUSED" || r.Label == "NO-EVIDENCE" {
		return fmt.Errorf("the session was refused: %v", r.Refuse)
	}
	return nil
}

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# The free rehearsal of the GPU fault-reaction study, on kind, with the gpu-simulator's fake DCGM.
#
#   bash hack/gpu-fault-reaction.sh            # the registered five blocks, about 75 minutes
#   GF_BLOCKS=3 bash hack/gpu-fault-reaction.sh
#
# docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-gpu-fault-reaction-design.md is the registration. This builds kind cluster
# `gpufault` (context kind-gpufault) from this tree, runs the smoke cell, the arms and the two stale checks through
# hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh -- the same procedure the paid instance runs -- scores it, and deletes the cluster (KEEP=1
# keeps it). It proves the apparatus, the scorer and the refusals; the fake is not DCGM, and the scorer labels the
# result REHEARSAL from the recorded mode, so nothing here can be read as a measurement of DCGM.
set -uo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." || exit 1

CLUSTER=gpufault
TAG=gpufault
JOB_IMAGE=busybox:1.36
OUT=${OUT:-hack/gpu-fault-rehearsal-$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)}
[ ! -e "$OUT" ] || { echo "$OUT exists; each rehearsal writes a new directory" >&2; exit 1; }
mkdir -p "$OUT/reps" "$OUT/checks" || exit 1

# The paid session's topology: the worker's card is faulted, and the control-plane is the healthy node.
export GF_MODE=fake GF_OUT=$OUT GF_TARGET=$CLUSTER-worker GF_HEALTHY=$CLUSTER-control-plane
export GF_SYS=gpu-platform-control-plane-system GF_NS=gpufault GF_EXPORTER=app.kubernetes.io/component=gpu-simulator
export GF_JOB_IMAGE=$JOB_IMAGE GF_CONTEXT=kind-$CLUSTER GF_LOG=$OUT/log.txt GF_ARMS_END=""
export GF_EMPTY_BODY=$OUT/empty-body.json
echo '{}' >"$GF_EMPTY_BODY"
# shellcheck source=hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh
. hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh

cleanup() {
  [ "${KEEP:-0}" = 1 ] && { gf_log "cleanup: KEEP=1, leaving $CLUSTER"; return; }
  local listed
  listed=$(kind get clusters 2>>"$GF_LOG") || gf_log "cleanup: kind get clusters failed"
  if grep -qx "$CLUSTER" <<<"$listed"; then
    kind delete cluster --name "$CLUSTER" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || gf_log "cleanup: kind delete failed"
    if grep -qx "$CLUSTER" <<<"$(kind get clusters 2>>"$GF_LOG")"; then gf_log "cleanup: $CLUSTER STILL EXISTS"; else gf_log "cleanup: deleted $CLUSTER"; fi
  fi
}
trap cleanup EXIT
die() { gf_log "REHEARSAL VOID: $*"; echo "$*" >"$OUT/VOID"; { gfk get pods -A -o wide; gfk get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -60; } >>"$OUT/failure-state.txt" 2>&1; exit 1; }

for tool in docker kind kubectl python3 go; do command -v "$tool" >/dev/null || die "missing tool $tool"; done
{
  echo "commit=$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
  echo "dirty_files=$(git status --porcelain | wc -l)"
  echo "mode=$GF_MODE"
  echo "kind=$(kind version)"
  echo "kueue=$(grep '^version=' config/kueue-controller/RELEASE | cut -d= -f2)"
  echo "blocks=${GF_BLOCKS:-5}"
} >"$OUT/provenance.txt"

make docker-build IMG=controller:$TAG >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "operator image"
make docker-build-gpu-simulator GPU_SIMULATOR_IMG=gpu-simulator:$TAG >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "simulator image"
docker image inspect "$JOB_IMAGE" >/dev/null 2>&1 || docker pull "$JOB_IMAGE" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "pull $JOB_IMAGE"
for img in controller:$TAG gpu-simulator:$TAG "$JOB_IMAGE"; do
  echo "image $img=$(docker image inspect "$img" --format '{{.Id}}')" >>"$OUT/provenance.txt"
done

gf_log "== cluster $CLUSTER"
if grep -qx "$CLUSTER" <<<"$(kind get clusters 2>>"$GF_LOG")"; then die "a cluster named $CLUSTER already exists; delete it first"; fi
kind create cluster --name "$CLUSTER" --config hack/kind-config-gpufault.yaml >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "kind create"
for img in controller:$TAG gpu-simulator:$TAG "$JOB_IMAGE"; do
  kind load docker-image "$img" --name "$CLUSTER" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "kind load $img"
done
# The healthy node takes workloads only once its control-plane taint is gone, as on the paid instance.
gfk taint node "$GF_HEALTHY" node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane:NoSchedule- >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "untaint $GF_HEALTHY"

gf_log "== queueing and the fake device plugin with its fake DCGM"
gf_install_queueing || die "queueing"
gfk kustomize config/device-plugin >"$OUT/device-plugin.yaml" || die "device plugin render"
gfk apply -f "$OUT/device-plugin.yaml" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "device plugin"
gfk -n "$GF_SYS" set image ds/gpu-simulator "*=gpu-simulator:$TAG" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "simulator image set"
gfk -n "$GF_SYS" patch ds/gpu-simulator --type=json -p '[
  {"op":"add","path":"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/imagePullPolicy","value":"IfNotPresent"},
  {"op":"add","path":"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/env/-","value":{"name":"FAKE_DCGM_ADDR","value":":9400"}},
  {"op":"add","path":"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/env/-","value":{"name":"NODE_NAME","valueFrom":{"fieldRef":{"fieldPath":"spec.nodeName"}}}}
]' >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "simulator fake DCGM"
gfk -n "$GF_SYS" rollout status ds/gpu-simulator --timeout=180s >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || die "simulator rollout"
for n in "$GF_TARGET" "$GF_HEALTHY"; do
  gf_wait_until 60 sh -c "[ \"\$(kubectl --context $GF_CONTEXT get node $n -o jsonpath='{.status.allocatable.nvidia\\.com/gpu}')\" = 1 ]" ||
    die "$n does not advertise one nvidia.com/gpu"
done

gf_log "== smoke cell, before the operator exists"
gf_smoke || die "smoke cell stopped the session: $(grep '^stop=' "$OUT/smoke.env")"

gf_log "== operator and tenant"
gf_deploy_operator controller:$TAG || die "operator"
gf_tenant || die "tenant"

gf_log "== arms"
gf_arms
gf_log "== stale checks"
gf_stale_checks || gf_log "the stale checks did not complete"

gf_log "== score"
go run ./cmd/benchharness gpu-fault-verdict --dir "$OUT" --json "$OUT/verdict.json" | tee "$OUT/verdict.txt"
gf_log "rehearsal evidence in $OUT"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# The GPU fault-reaction study's instance side: run by the user-data hack/gpu-fault-session.sh renders, on the kind
# cluster `m5cgpu` the spliced bring-up created, from the unpacked source tree.
#
# Inputs, from the user-data: KUBECONFIG; GF_OUT; and the phase deadlines GF_PLATFORM_END, GF_ARMS_END and
# GF_CHECKS_END, epoch seconds counted from EC2's LaunchTime. Images come from /src/images, checksummed by the
# user-data before this runs.
#
# The procedure is hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh's, the one the rehearsal runs; what differs is only the exporter (the study
# overlay with a real host engine), the healthy node (the control-plane with a simulated device) and the deadlines.
set -uo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." || exit 1

: "${GF_OUT:?}" "${GF_PLATFORM_END:?}" "${GF_ARMS_END:?}" "${GF_CHECKS_END:?}"
mkdir -p "$GF_OUT/reps" "$GF_OUT/checks" || exit 1
export GF_MODE=dcgm GF_TARGET=m5cgpu-worker GF_HEALTHY=m5cgpu-control-plane GF_CONTEXT=""
export GF_SYS=gpu-platform-control-plane-system GF_NS=gpufault GF_EXPORTER=app.kubernetes.io/component=dcgm-exporter
export GF_JOB_IMAGE=busybox:1.36 GF_LOG=$GF_OUT/log.txt GF_EMPTY_BODY=$GF_OUT/empty-body.json
echo '{}' >"$GF_EMPTY_BODY"
# shellcheck source=hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh
. hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh

phase() { echo "$1=$2" >>"$GF_OUT/phases.env"; gf_log "phase $1: $2"; }
stop() { phase "$1" "stopped: $2"; exit 1; }
before() { [ "$(date +%s)" -lt "$1" ]; }

{
  echo "mode=$GF_MODE"
  echo "kubectl=$(kubectl version --client -o json | python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)["clientVersion"]["gitVersion"])')"
  echo "node_image=$(docker inspect m5cgpu-worker --format '{{.Config.Image}}')"
  echo "kueue=$(grep '^version=' config/kueue-controller/RELEASE | cut -d= -f2)"
  echo "exporter_image=$(grep -m1 -oE 'image: *[^ ]+' config/dcgm-exporter/daemonset.yaml | sed 's/image: *//')"
  echo "device_plugin_image=$(grep -m1 -oE 'image: *[^ ]+' config/nvidia-device-plugin/daemonset.yaml | sed 's/image: *//')"
} >>"$GF_OUT/provenance.txt"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- platform
for n in controller gpu-simulator job; do
  docker load -i "/src/images/$n.tar.gz" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "docker load $n"
done
for img in controller:gpufault gpu-simulator:gpufault "$GF_JOB_IMAGE"; do
  kind load docker-image "$img" --name m5cgpu >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "kind load $img"
  echo "image $img=$(docker image inspect "$img" --format '{{.Id}}')" >>"$GF_OUT/provenance.txt"
done
gfk label node "$GF_TARGET" platform.lkhun9311.github.io/gpu=true --overwrite >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "label $GF_TARGET"
gfk taint node "$GF_HEALTHY" node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane:NoSchedule- >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "untaint $GF_HEALTHY"
gf_install_queueing || stop platform "queueing"

# The real card on T, through NVIDIA's device plugin.
gfk create ns "$GF_SYS" --dry-run=client -o yaml | gfk apply -f - >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1
gfk kustomize config/nvidia-device-plugin | gfk apply -f - >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "nvidia device plugin"
# One simulated device on H, and only on H: two plugins registering nvidia.com/gpu on T would replace each other.
gfk kustomize config/device-plugin >"$GF_OUT/device-plugin.yaml" || stop platform "render gpu-simulator"
gfk apply -f "$GF_OUT/device-plugin.yaml" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "gpu-simulator"
gfk -n "$GF_SYS" patch ds/gpu-simulator --type=json -p "[
  {\"op\":\"replace\",\"path\":\"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/image\",\"value\":\"gpu-simulator:gpufault\"},
  {\"op\":\"add\",\"path\":\"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/imagePullPolicy\",\"value\":\"IfNotPresent\"},
  {\"op\":\"add\",\"path\":\"/spec/template/spec/nodeSelector\",\"value\":{\"kubernetes.io/hostname\":\"$GF_HEALTHY\"}}
]" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "pin gpu-simulator to $GF_HEALTHY"
for n in "$GF_TARGET" "$GF_HEALTHY"; do
  gf_wait_until 180 sh -c "[ \"\$(kubectl get node $n -o jsonpath='{.status.allocatable.nvidia\\.com/gpu}')\" = 1 ]" ||
    stop platform "$n does not advertise one nvidia.com/gpu"
done
gfk kustomize config/dcgm-exporter-fault >"$GF_OUT/exporter.yaml" || stop platform "render the study exporter"
gfk apply -f "$GF_OUT/exporter.yaml" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || stop platform "the study exporter"

before "$GF_PLATFORM_END" || stop platform "out of time before the smoke cell"
gf_smoke || stop smoke "$(grep '^stop=' "$GF_OUT/smoke.env")"
phase smoke done
before "$GF_PLATFORM_END" || stop platform "out of time before the operator"
gf_deploy_operator controller:gpufault || stop platform "operator"
gf_tenant || stop platform "tenant"
phase platform done

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- arms and checks
gf_arms
phase arms done
if [ $(( $(date +%s) + 240 )) -le "$GF_CHECKS_END" ]; then
  gf_stale_checks || gf_log "the stale checks did not complete"
  phase checks done
else
  phase checks "not run: fewer than 240 s left before $GF_CHECKS_END"
fi
#!/usr/bin/env bash
#
# The GPU fault-reaction session: one g5.2xlarge Spot instance, one attempt, stopped by itself 7,000 s after launch.
#
#   SESSION_CONFIRM=yes AWS_PROFILE=gpu-lab GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID=ami-... bash hack/gpu-fault-session.sh
#
# docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-gpu-fault-reaction-design.md is the registration; this script is the runner it
# names. It is modelled line for line on hack/mps-capability-session.sh and shares what that runner shares: the EC2
# lifecycle from hack/lib/spot-run.sh, and the instance bring-up spliced from hack/m5c-gpu-session.sh between its two
# anchor lines. The cell procedure is hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh, the file the free rehearsal runs, driven on the instance
# by hack/gpu-fault-instance.sh.
#
# THE CLOCK. Every deadline counts from EC2's LaunchTime, read right after the launch and written to the run's S3
# prefix for the instance to read. The instance's phases end at 720, 1,320, 6,520 and 6,820 s after it, its evidence
# at 6,940 s, and it shuts itself down at 7,000 s. The launch carries the termination sweeper's study-deadline tag
# at the request plus 6,940 s, rewritten to LaunchTime plus 6,970 s once LaunchTime is known, so the sweeper cannot
# cut the evidence step.
#
# NOTHING HERE IS A DEFAULT A CALLER CAN MOVE: the registration fixes the instance, the arms and the stop, so each is
# a constant, and a caller who sets one is refused rather than ignored.
set -uo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." || exit 1

REGION=ap-northeast-2
ACCOUNT_WANT=007635145730
INSTANCE_TYPE_FIXED=g5.2xlarge
# The repository's worst-case rate for this instance: Spot cap $1.10, 200 GB gp3, a public IPv4
# (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-measuring-prospective-admission-design.md).
MAX_PRICE_FIXED=1.1324
STUDY_ID=gpu-fault-2026-10-27
STACK=gpufault-gpu
PURPOSE=gpu-fault
TAG_AT_LAUNCH_S=6940
TAG_FROM_LAUNCH_S=6970
STOP_FROM_LAUNCH_S=7000
PHASE_BRINGUP_S=720
PHASE_PLATFORM_S=1320
PHASE_ARMS_S=6520
PHASE_CHECKS_S=6820
EVIDENCE_S=6940
TERMINATION_S=7300
LAUNCHTIME_WAIT_S=180
SWEEPER_MIN_RUNS=4
# The session's end of the termination wait plus 30 minutes of headroom, in minutes.
CRED_MIN_MINUTES=155
TAG=gpufault
JOB_IMAGE=busybox:1.36

OUT="${OUT:-hack/gpu-fault-$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)}"
say() { echo "== $*" | { if [ -d "$OUT" ]; then tee -a "$OUT/session.log"; else cat; fi; }; }
fail() { echo "SESSION REFUSED: $*" | { if [ -d "$OUT" ]; then tee -a "$OUT/session.log"; else cat; fi; } >&2; exit 1; }
spot_say() { say "$@"; }
spot_fail() { fail "$@"; }

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- refusals, before anything is built or bought
for v in GF_BLOCKS GF_ARMS_END GF_MODE INSTANCE_TYPE MAX_SPOT_PRICE HARD_STOP_SECONDS REPS ARMS; do
  [ -z "${!v:-}" ] || fail "$v is set, and this session's value for it is its registration's; unset it"
done
[ "${SESSION_CONFIRM:-}" = yes ] || fail "SESSION_CONFIRM=yes is required: this buys a GPU instance, and only the owner's approval starts one"
[[ "${GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID:-}" =~ ^ami-[0-9a-f]{8,17}$ ]] ||
  fail "GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID is ${GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID:-unset}; the registration pins the AMI by ID rather than resolving it from latest"
for tool in aws go python3 git tar sha256sum docker make gzip; do command -v "$tool" >/dev/null || fail "$tool is not installed"; done
[ -z "$(git status --porcelain)" ] || fail "the working tree is dirty; the run's provenance is a commit, and the images are built from this tree"
COMMIT=$(git rev-parse HEAD)
ACCOUNT=$(aws sts get-caller-identity --query Account --output text 2>&1) || fail "could not read the caller's account: $ACCOUNT"
[ "$ACCOUNT" = "$ACCOUNT_WANT" ] || fail "the credentials are for account $ACCOUNT; paid sessions run only in gpu-lab ($ACCOUNT_WANT)"
[ ! -e "$OUT" ] || fail "$OUT already exists; each session writes a new directory"
mkdir -p "$OUT" || fail "cannot create $OUT"
say "the GPU fault-reaction session, $STUDY_ID, from $COMMIT, into $OUT"

# shellcheck source=hack/lib/spot-run.sh
. hack/lib/spot-run.sh

ami_name=$(aws ec2 describe-images --region "$REGION" --image-ids "$GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID" \
  --query 'Images[0].[Name,State]' --output text 2>&1) || fail "could not describe $GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID: $ami_name"
case "$ami_name" in
  "Deep Learning Base OSS Nvidia Driver GPU AMI (Ubuntu 22.04)"*available) ;;
  *) fail "$GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID is '$ami_name', not an available Deep Learning Base OSS Nvidia Driver GPU AMI (Ubuntu 22.04)" ;;
esac
say "AMI $GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID: $ami_name"

prices=$(aws ec2 describe-spot-price-history --region "$REGION" --instance-types "$INSTANCE_TYPE_FIXED" \
  --product-descriptions "Linux/UNIX" --start-time "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" \
  --query 'SpotPriceHistory[].[AvailabilityZone,SpotPrice]' --output text 2>&1) || fail "could not read the Spot price: $prices"
printf '%s\n' "$prices" >"$OUT/spot-prices.txt"
ZONES=$(awk -v cap="$MAX_PRICE_FIXED" 'NF == 2 && $2 + 0 > 0 && $2 + 0 <= cap + 0 {print $1}' <<<"$prices" | sort -u | paste -sd' ')
[ -n "$ZONES" ] || fail "no zone offers $INSTANCE_TYPE_FIXED Spot at or under \$$MAX_PRICE_FIXED/h: $(paste -sd' ' <<<"$prices")"
say "Spot $INSTANCE_TYPE_FIXED under \$$MAX_PRICE_FIXED/h in: $ZONES ($(paste -sd' ' <<<"$prices"))"

quota=$(aws service-quotas get-service-quota --region "$REGION" --service-code ec2 --quota-code L-3819A6DF \
  --query 'Quota.Value' --output text 2>&1) || fail "could not read the G and VT Spot quota: $quota"
python3 -c 'import sys; sys.exit(0 if float(sys.argv[1]) >= 8 else 1)' "$quota" ||
  fail "the G and VT Spot quota is $quota vCPU and one $INSTANCE_TYPE_FIXED needs 8"

sw_state=$(aws lambda get-function --region "$REGION" --function-name gpu-platform-study-sweeper \
  --query 'Configuration.State' --output text 2>&1) || fail "the termination sweeper could not be read: $sw_state"
[ "$sw_state" = Active ] || fail "the termination sweeper is $sw_state, not Active"
sw_rule=$(aws events describe-rule --region "$REGION" --name gpu-platform-study-sweeper \
  --query '[State,ScheduleExpression]' --output text 2>&1) || fail "the sweeper's rule could not be read: $sw_rule"
[ "$(tr -s '[:space:]' ' ' <<<"$sw_rule" | sed 's/ $//')" = "ENABLED rate(5 minutes)" ] || fail "the sweeper's rule is '$sw_rule', not ENABLED rate(5 minutes)"
sw_target=$(aws events list-targets-by-rule --region "$REGION" --rule gpu-platform-study-sweeper \
  --query 'Targets[].Arn' --output text 2>&1) || fail "the sweeper's targets could not be read: $sw_target"
[ "$sw_target" = "arn:aws:lambda:$REGION:$ACCOUNT_WANT:function:gpu-platform-study-sweeper" ] ||
  fail "the sweeper's rule targets '$sw_target', not the sweeper"
sw_metric() {
  aws cloudwatch get-metric-statistics --region "$REGION" --namespace AWS/Lambda --metric-name "$1" \
    --dimensions Name=FunctionName,Value=gpu-platform-study-sweeper \
    --start-time "$(date -u -d '-30 min' +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" --end-time "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" \
    --period 1800 --statistics Sum --query 'Datapoints[0].Sum' --output text
}
sw_runs=$(sw_metric Invocations 2>&1) || fail "the sweeper's invocations could not be read: $sw_runs"
sw_errors=$(sw_metric Errors 2>&1) || fail "the sweeper's errors could not be read: $sw_errors"
python3 -c 'import sys; r, e, least = sys.argv[1:4]; sys.exit(0 if r != "None" and float(r) >= int(least) and e in ("None", "0.0", "0") else 1)' \
  "$sw_runs" "$sw_errors" "$SWEEPER_MIN_RUNS" ||
  fail "the sweeper ran $sw_runs time(s) with $sw_errors error(s) in the last 30 minutes; at least $SWEEPER_MIN_RUNS clean runs are required"
say "the termination sweeper: Active, ENABLED rate(5 minutes), $sw_runs run(s) and ${sw_errors/None/0} error(s) in the last 30 minutes"

cred_json=$(aws configure export-credentials --format process 2>&1) || fail "the CLI could not export the active credential: $cred_json"
cred_exp=$(python3 -c 'import json,sys; print(json.loads(sys.argv[1]).get("Expiration",""))' "$cred_json") ||
  fail "the exported credential is not JSON"
[ -n "$cred_exp" ] || fail "the exported credential carries no expiry, so it cannot be shown to outlast the session"
cred_left=$(python3 -c 'import datetime,sys; e=datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(sys.argv[1].replace("Z","+00:00")); print(int((e-datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)).total_seconds()//60))' "$cred_exp")
[ "$cred_left" -ge "$CRED_MIN_MINUTES" ] ||
  fail "the credentials expire in $cred_left minutes; the session needs $CRED_MIN_MINUTES. Re-authenticate first"
say "credentials expire in $cred_left minutes"

live=$(aws ec2 describe-instances --region "$REGION" --filters "Name=tag:purpose,Values=$PURPOSE" \
  "Name=instance-state-name,Values=pending,running,shutting-down,stopping,stopped" \
  --query 'Reservations[].Instances[].InstanceId' --output text 2>&1) || fail "could not list earlier instances: $live"
[ -z "$live" ] || fail "an earlier fault-reaction instance is still live ($live); one attempt is registered"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- what the instance runs
say "building the operator, the simulator and the scorer from $COMMIT"
make docker-build IMG=controller:$TAG >"$OUT/build.log" 2>&1 || fail "operator image; see $OUT/build.log"
make docker-build-gpu-simulator GPU_SIMULATOR_IMG=gpu-simulator:$TAG >>"$OUT/build.log" 2>&1 || fail "simulator image; see $OUT/build.log"
docker pull "$JOB_IMAGE" >>"$OUT/build.log" 2>&1 || fail "could not pull $JOB_IMAGE"
mkdir -p "$OUT/images" || fail "mkdir images"
for pair in controller:$TAG:controller gpu-simulator:$TAG:gpu-simulator "$JOB_IMAGE:job"; do
  img=${pair%:*} name=${pair##*:}
  docker save "$img" | gzip -1 >"$OUT/images/$name.tar.gz" || fail "could not save $img"
done
go build -o "$OUT/benchharness-local" ./cmd/benchharness || fail "build the local scorer"
git archive --format=tar.gz -o "$OUT/source.tgz" HEAD || fail "git archive"
SOURCE_SHA=$(sha256sum "$OUT/source.tgz" | cut -d' ' -f1)
IMAGES_SHA=$(cd "$OUT/images" && sha256sum controller.tar.gz gpu-simulator.tar.gz job.tar.gz | sha256sum | cut -d' ' -f1)
RUN_NONCE=$(python3 -c 'import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(4))')
RUN_ID="$(basename "$OUT")-$RUN_NONCE"
BUCKET="$STACK-$ACCOUNT"
{
  echo "commit=$COMMIT"
  echo "study=$STUDY_ID"
  echo "ami_id=$GPU_FAULT_AMI_ID"
  echo "ami_name=$ami_name"
  echo "instance_type=$INSTANCE_TYPE_FIXED"
  echo "max_spot_price=$MAX_PRICE_FIXED"
  echo "source_sha256=$SOURCE_SHA"
  echo "images_sha256=$IMAGES_SHA"
  for name in controller gpu-simulator job; do echo "image_$name=$(sha256sum "$OUT/images/$name.tar.gz" | cut -d' ' -f1)"; done
  echo "run_id=$RUN_ID"
} >"$OUT/session-provenance.txt"

bringup_from=$(grep -nx 'export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive' hack/m5c-gpu-session.sh | cut -d: -f1 | paste -sd' ')
bringup_to=$(grep -nx 'upload /tmp/node-cards.txt preflight-node-cards.txt' hack/m5c-gpu-session.sh | cut -d: -f1 | paste -sd' ')
[[ "$bringup_from" =~ ^[0-9]+$ && "$bringup_to" =~ ^[0-9]+$ && "$bringup_from" -lt "$bringup_to" ]] ||
  fail "the bring-up anchors in hack/m5c-gpu-session.sh are not each found once, in order (from '$bringup_from', to '$bringup_to')"
sed -n "${bringup_from},${bringup_to}p" hack/m5c-gpu-session.sh >"$OUT/bringup.sh"

UD="$OUT/user-data.sh"
{
  cat <<'HEAD'
#!/bin/bash
exec > >(tee /var/log/gpufault.log) 2>&1
set -x
GF_START=$(date +%s)
# The first backstop, armed before anything can fail, counts from the instance's own start; LaunchTime precedes it,
# so the precise one below, armed once LaunchTime is read, always fires first.
( sleep STOP_FROM_LAUNCH_S_PLACEHOLDER; shutdown -h now ) &
BUCKET="BUCKET_PLACEHOLDER"
PREFIX="RUN_ID_PLACEHOLDER"
SOURCE_SHA="SOURCE_SHA_PLACEHOLDER"
IMAGES_SHA="IMAGES_SHA_PLACEHOLDER"
COMMIT="COMMIT_PLACEHOLDER"
upload() { aws s3 cp --only-show-errors "$1" "s3://$BUCKET/$PREFIX/$2" || true; }
trap 'upload /var/log/gpufault.log log.txt; shutdown -h now' EXIT
aws s3 cp --only-show-errors "s3://$BUCKET/$PREFIX/src/source.tgz" /tmp/source.tgz
[ "$(sha256sum /tmp/source.tgz | cut -d' ' -f1)" = "$SOURCE_SHA" ] || { echo "source checksum mismatch"; exit 1; }
mkdir -p /src && tar -xzf /tmp/source.tgz -C /src && cd /src || exit 1
mkdir -p /src/images /src/gf-run
for n in controller gpu-simulator job; do aws s3 cp --only-show-errors "s3://$BUCKET/$PREFIX/images/$n.tar.gz" "/src/images/$n.tar.gz"; done
[ "$(cd /src/images && sha256sum controller.tar.gz gpu-simulator.tar.gz job.tar.gz | sha256sum | cut -d' ' -f1)" = "$IMAGES_SHA" ] ||
  { echo "image checksum mismatch"; exit 1; }
L=""
while [ $(( $(date +%s) - GF_START )) -lt LAUNCHTIME_WAIT_S_PLACEHOLDER ]; do
  if aws s3 cp --only-show-errors "s3://$BUCKET/$PREFIX/launch-time.txt" /tmp/launch-time.txt; then
    L=$(date -u -d "$(cat /tmp/launch-time.txt)" +%s || true)
    break
  fi
  sleep 5
done
NOW=$(date +%s)
if ! [[ "$L" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || [ "$L" -gt "$NOW" ] || [ $(( NOW - L )) -gt 900 ]; then
  echo "LaunchTime was ${L:-not read} at $NOW"; exit 1
fi
( sleep $(( L + STOP_FROM_LAUNCH_S_PLACEHOLDER - $(date +%s) > 0 ? L + STOP_FROM_LAUNCH_S_PLACEHOLDER - $(date +%s) : 1 )); shutdown -h now ) &
P1=$(( L + PHASE_BRINGUP_S_PLACEHOLDER )); P2=$(( L + PHASE_PLATFORM_S_PLACEHOLDER )); P3=$(( L + PHASE_ARMS_S_PLACEHOLDER )); P4=$(( L + PHASE_CHECKS_S_PLACEHOLDER )); EV=$(( L + EVIDENCE_S_PLACEHOLDER ))
nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,name,memory.total,driver_version --format=csv > /tmp/nvidia-smi.csv || exit 1
upload /tmp/nvidia-smi.csv preflight-nvidia-smi.csv
[ "$(tail -n +2 /tmp/nvidia-smi.csv | wc -l)" = 1 ] && tail -n +2 /tmp/nvidia-smi.csv | grep -q A10G || { echo "PREFLIGHT FAILED: not one A10G"; exit 1; }
HEAD
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  echo "cat > /tmp/bringup.sh <<'BRINGUP'"
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  echo 'upload() { aws s3 cp --only-show-errors "$1" "s3://$BUCKET/$PREFIX/$2" || true; }'
  cat "$OUT/bringup.sh"
  echo "BRINGUP"
  cat <<'TAIL'
export BUCKET PREFIX
bringup_left=$(( P1 - $(date +%s) ))
bringup_rc=124
if [ "$bringup_left" -gt 0 ]; then
  timeout --kill-after=15 "$bringup_left" bash /tmp/bringup.sh
  bringup_rc=$?
fi
echo "bring-up exited $bringup_rc"
{
  echo "commit=$COMMIT"
  echo "launch_time_epoch=$L"
  echo "driver=$(tail -n +2 /tmp/nvidia-smi.csv | cut -d, -f4 | tr -d ' ')"
  echo "bringup_rc=$bringup_rc"
} > /src/gf-run/instance-provenance.txt
if [ "$bringup_rc" = 0 ]; then
  export KUBECONFIG=/tmp/kubeconfig GF_PLATFORM_END="$P2" GF_ARMS_END="$P3" GF_CHECKS_END="$P4" GF_OUT=/src/gf-run
  timeout --kill-after=15 $(( EV - $(date +%s) > 5 ? EV - $(date +%s) : 5 )) bash hack/gpu-fault-instance.sh
  echo "the instance procedure exited $?"
else
  echo "the bring-up did not finish inside its phase (exit $bringup_rc); nothing else runs" | tee /src/gf-run/VOID
fi
left=$(( EV + 50 - $(date +%s) )); [ "$left" -gt 5 ] || left=5
tar -czf /tmp/evidence.tgz -C /src gf-run && timeout "$left" aws s3 cp --only-show-errors /tmp/evidence.tgz "s3://$BUCKET/$PREFIX/evidence.tgz"
upload /var/log/gpufault.log log.txt
echo "RUN_NONCE_PLACEHOLDER" > /tmp/DONE
upload /tmp/DONE DONE
TAIL
} >"$UD.template"
sed -e "s|BUCKET_PLACEHOLDER|$BUCKET|" -e "s|RUN_ID_PLACEHOLDER|$RUN_ID|" -e "s|SOURCE_SHA_PLACEHOLDER|$SOURCE_SHA|" \
  -e "s|IMAGES_SHA_PLACEHOLDER|$IMAGES_SHA|" -e "s|COMMIT_PLACEHOLDER|$COMMIT|" -e "s|RUN_NONCE_PLACEHOLDER|$RUN_NONCE|" \
  -e "s|LAUNCHTIME_WAIT_S_PLACEHOLDER|$LAUNCHTIME_WAIT_S|" -e "s|STOP_FROM_LAUNCH_S_PLACEHOLDER|$STOP_FROM_LAUNCH_S|g" \
  -e "s|PHASE_BRINGUP_S_PLACEHOLDER|$PHASE_BRINGUP_S|" -e "s|PHASE_PLATFORM_S_PLACEHOLDER|$PHASE_PLATFORM_S|" \
  -e "s|PHASE_ARMS_S_PLACEHOLDER|$PHASE_ARMS_S|" -e "s|PHASE_CHECKS_S_PLACEHOLDER|$PHASE_CHECKS_S|" \
  -e "s|EVIDENCE_S_PLACEHOLDER|$EVIDENCE_S|" "$UD.template" >"$UD" || fail "could not render the user-data"
rm -f "$UD.template"

/bin/bash -lc 'cat hack/lib/gpu-fault.sh' in /home/lkhun9311/workspace/gpu-platform-control-plane/.claude/worktrees/agent-a5b6768413de6a7da
 succeeded in 0ms:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
#
# The cell procedure of the GPU fault-reaction study, shared by the free rehearsal on kind
# (hack/gpu-fault-reaction.sh) and the paid session's instance (hack/gpu-fault-instance.sh).
# docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-gpu-fault-reaction-design.md is the registration; every number below that
# is not derived there is a convention it names.
#
# WHAT THE CALLER PROVIDES
#   GF_MODE        fake (the gpu-simulator's fake DCGM) or dcgm (the study exporter with its host engine)
#   GF_OUT         the session directory; repetitions go to $GF_OUT/reps, checks to $GF_OUT/checks
#   GF_TARGET      the node whose card is faulted
#   GF_HEALTHY     the node the displaced job must land on
#   GF_SYS         the namespace of the exporter and the operator
#   GF_NS          the training namespace, whose GPUQuotaPolicy gives a ClusterQueue of one GPU
#   GF_EXPORTER    the label selector of the exporter Pods
#   GF_JOB_IMAGE   the job's image, already on the nodes
#   GF_CONTEXT     a kube context, or empty for the current KUBECONFIG
#   GF_ARMS_END    epoch seconds by which the last repetition must have finished, or empty for none
#   GF_LOG         the session log
#
# Like hack/lib/spot-run.sh it never calls `set`, never `cd`s and installs no traps: those belong to the caller.
# Every function returns non-zero on failure and says why on stderr.

# The registered conventions, each named in the registration with its reason.
GF_WINDOW_S=120
GF_SETTLE_S=10
GF_START_S=60
GF_RESET_S=60
GF_SENTINEL_S=10
GF_OFFSET_S=900
GF_PROBE_PERIOD=0.2
GF_STALE_WAIT_S=15
GF_LATCH_WAIT_S=10
# One repetition's longest path: start, settle, window, sentinel, reset.
GF_REP_BUDGET_S=$((GF_START_S + GF_SETTLE_S + GF_WINDOW_S + GF_SENTINEL_S + GF_RESET_S))

# Field IDs (dcgmlib/dcgm_fields.h) and the exporter's names for them.
declare -A GF_FIELD_NAME=([230]=DCGM_FI_DEV_XID_ERRORS [311]=DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_DBE_VOL_TOTAL [310]=DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_SBE_VOL_TOTAL)
declare -A GF_ARM_FIELD=([xid]=230 [ecc]=311 [benign]=310 [none]=230)
declare -A GF_ARM_VALUE=([xid]=79 [ecc]=1 [benign]=1 [none]=)

gf_log() { echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) $*" | tee -a "${GF_LOG:-/dev/stderr}" >&2; }
gf_now() { date +%s.%N; }
gfk() {
  if [ -n "${GF_CONTEXT:-}" ]; then kubectl --context "$GF_CONTEXT" "$@"; else kubectl "$@"; fi
}

# gf_exporter_pod NODE echoes the one Running exporter Pod on NODE, and fails unless there is exactly one.
gf_exporter_pod() {
  local listed pods
  # A terminating Pod is still Running, and reading it would read an exporter on its way out.
  listed=$(gfk -n "$GF_SYS" get pods -l "$GF_EXPORTER" --field-selector "spec.nodeName=$1,status.phase=Running" \
    -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.metadata.name}{" "}{.metadata.deletionTimestamp}{"\n"}{end}') || return 1
  pods=$(awk 'NF == 1 {print $1}' <<<"$listed")
  [ -n "$pods" ] && [ "$(wc -l <<<"$pods")" = 1 ] || { echo "not exactly one exporter Pod on $1: '$listed'" >&2; return 1; }
  echo "$pods"
}

gf_proxy() { echo "/api/v1/namespaces/$GF_SYS/pods/$GF_POD:9400/proxy$1"; }

# gf_metrics echoes the target exporter's /metrics, through the apiserver's Pod proxy.
gf_metrics() { gfk get --raw "$(gf_proxy /metrics)"; }

# gf_exported FIELD echoes the value the exporter serves for FIELD on the target card, or fails.
gf_exported() {
  local body v
  body=$(gf_metrics) || return 1
  v=$(awk -v n="${GF_FIELD_NAME[$1]}" -v u="UUID=\"$GF_UUID\"" 'index($0, n "{") == 1 && index($0, u) {print $NF; exit}' <<<"$body")
  [ -n "$v" ] || return 1
  echo "$v"
}

# gf_inject FIELD VALUE writes into DCGM's cache, always at the registered fixed offset into the future.
gf_inject() {
  case "$GF_MODE" in
    fake)
      gfk create --raw "$(gf_proxy "/inject?gpu=0&field=$1&value=$2&offset=$GF_OFFSET_S")" -f "$GF_EMPTY_BODY" >/dev/null ;;
    dcgm)
      gfk -n "$GF_SYS" exec "$GF_POD" -c hostengine -- dcgmi test --inject --gpuid 0 -f "$1" -v "$2" --offset "$GF_OFFSET_S" ;;
    *) echo "GF_MODE '$GF_MODE'" >&2; return 1 ;;
  esac
}

# gf_readback FIELD echoes the value DCGM returns for FIELD on GPU 0.
#
# On a real host engine it is `dcgmi dmon`, not `dcgmi test --introspect`: introspection prints the cache's
# metadata (newest and oldest timestamps, fetch counts), not the value (astra review, finding 1, re-derived from
# the pinned binary's strings). dmon prints one row per entity, "GPU 0  <value>", which is what is parsed.
gf_readback() {
  case "$GF_MODE" in
    fake) gfk get --raw "$(gf_proxy "/introspect?gpu=0&field=$1")" | tr -d '[:space:]' ;;
    dcgm)
      local out
      out=$(gfk -n "$GF_SYS" exec "$GF_POD" -c hostengine -- dcgmi dmon -e "$1" -i 0 -c 1) || return 1
      printf '%s\n' "$out" >>"$GF_OUT/dmon-raw.log"
      awk '$1 == "GPU" && $2 == "0" {v = $3} END {if (v == "") exit 1; print v}' <<<"$out" ;;
  esac
}

# gf_exporter_state up|down takes the target's exporter away, or brings it back.
gf_exporter_state() {
  case "$GF_MODE" in
    fake) gfk create --raw "$(gf_proxy "/exporter?state=$1")" -f "$GF_EMPTY_BODY" >/dev/null ;;
    dcgm)
      # The DaemonSet's Pod leaves the node when its own selector stops matching, which is how an exporter
      # leaves. The node's label is not touched: the device plugin selects on it too, and taking the plugin away
      # would change allocatable and so the very boundary P5 records.
      if [ "$1" = down ]; then
        gfk -n "$GF_SYS" patch ds dcgm-exporter --type=json \
          -p '[{"op":"add","path":"/spec/template/spec/nodeSelector/gpufault-study~1exporter","value":"away"}]' >/dev/null || return 1
        gfk -n "$GF_SYS" wait --for=delete "pod/$GF_POD" --timeout=60s >/dev/null || return 1
      else
        gfk -n "$GF_SYS" patch ds dcgm-exporter --type=json \
          -p '[{"op":"remove","path":"/spec/template/spec/nodeSelector/gpufault-study~1exporter"}]' >/dev/null || return 1
        gf_wait_exporter || return 1
      fi ;;
  esac
}

# gf_wait_exporter waits for the target's exporter Pod, records it in GF_POD, and waits for it to serve the card.
gf_wait_exporter() {
  local i
  for i in $(seq 1 60); do
    if GF_POD=$(gf_exporter_pod "$GF_TARGET" 2>/dev/null) &&
      gfk -n "$GF_SYS" wait --for=condition=Ready "pod/$GF_POD" --timeout=5s >/dev/null 2>&1 &&
      gf_metrics 2>/dev/null | grep -q "^${GF_FIELD_NAME[230]}{"; then
      export GF_POD
      return 0
    fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo "the exporter on $GF_TARGET never served ${GF_FIELD_NAME[230]} (attempt $i)" >&2
  return 1
}

gf_allocatable() { gfk get node "$GF_TARGET" -o jsonpath='{.status.allocatable.nvidia\.com/gpu}'; }

# gf_nh_field NODE JSONPATH reads the NodeHealth whose spec.nodeName is NODE.
gf_nh_field() {
  gfk get nodehealths -o json | python3 -c '
import json, sys
node, path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
items = [i for i in json.load(sys.stdin)["items"] if i["spec"]["nodeName"] == node]
if len(items) != 1:
    sys.exit("not exactly one NodeHealth for " + node)
nh = items[0]
if path.startswith("cond:"):
    t, k = path[5:].split(".")
    c = [c for c in nh.get("status", {}).get("conditions", []) if c["type"] == t]
    print(c[0][k] if c else "")
elif path.startswith("ann:"):
    print(nh["metadata"].get("annotations", {}).get("gpufault.platform.lkhun9311.github.io/" + path[4:], ""))
' "$1" "$2"
}

gf_tainted() {
  gfk get node "$1" -o jsonpath='{range .spec.taints[*]}{.key}{"\n"}{end}' | grep -qx 'platform.lkhun9311.github.io/gpu-fault'
}

# gf_wait_until SECONDS CMD... polls CMD every half second until it succeeds or SECONDS pass.
gf_wait_until() {
  local until=$(( $(date +%s) + $1 )); shift
  while [ "$(date +%s)" -lt "$until" ]; do "$@" && return 0; sleep 0.5; done
  "$@"
}

gf_is_clear() {
  [ "$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" cond:GPUHealthy.status)" = True ] && [ "$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" cond:GPUSignal.status)" = True ] &&
    [ -z "$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" ann:fingerprint)" ] && ! gf_tainted "$GF_TARGET"
}

# gf_reset injects the baseline into every field the smoke cell passed and waits for the platform to clear.
gf_reset() {
  local f
  for f in $GF_FIELDS; do
    gf_inject "$f" 0 >/dev/null || { echo "reset: injecting $f=0 failed" >&2; return 1; }
  done
  [ -z "${GF_OPERATOR_UP:-}" ] && return 0
  gf_wait_until "$GF_RESET_S" gf_is_clear
}

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- watches
# gf_watch_start DIR JOB starts one watch per resource, each recording its start and end in watch-<name>.log.
# A watch that ended inside the window is a gap, and the scorer refuses the repetition for it.
gf_watch_start() {
  local dir=$1 job=$2 name
  GF_WATCH_PIDFILES=()
  for name in node nodehealth workload job pods; do
    local args=()
    case $name in
      node) args=(get node --field-selector "metadata.name=$GF_TARGET") ;;
      nodehealth) args=(get nodehealths) ;;
      workload) args=(-n "$GF_NS" get workloads.kueue.x-k8s.io) ;;
      job) args=(-n "$GF_NS" get jobs.batch --field-selector "metadata.name=$job") ;;
      pods) args=(-n "$GF_NS" get pods -l "batch.kubernetes.io/job-name=$job") ;;
    esac
    (
      echo "start $(gf_now)" >>"$dir/watch-$name.log"
      gfk "${args[@]}" -w -o json >"$dir/watch-$name.json" 2>>"$dir/watch-$name.err" &
      echo $! >"$dir/watch-$name.pid"
      wait $!
      echo "end $(gf_now) $?" >>"$dir/watch-$name.log"
    ) &
    GF_WATCH_PIDFILES+=("$dir/watch-$name.pid")
  done
  # Each watch must have delivered its initial objects before anything is injected, or its start is not before.
  for name in node nodehealth workload job pods; do
    gf_wait_until 20 test -s "$dir/watch-$name.json" || { echo "watch $name delivered nothing in 20 s" >&2; return 1; }
  done
}

gf_watch_stop() {
  local f
  for f in "${GF_WATCH_PIDFILES[@]}"; do
    [ -s "$f" ] && kill "$(cat "$f")" 2>>"$GF_LOG"
  done
  # Each watch's subshell writes its end record after its kubectl exits; wait for those, and nothing else.
  for f in "${GF_WATCH_PIDFILES[@]}"; do
    gf_wait_until 10 grep -q '^end ' "${f%.pid}.log" || echo "watch ${f%.pid} wrote no end record" >&2
  done
  return 0
}

# gf_probe_start FIELD FILE samples the exporter's value for FIELD every GF_PROBE_PERIOD seconds into FILE.
gf_probe_start() {
  local field=$1 file=$2
  (
    while :; do
      v=$(gf_exported "$field" 2>/dev/null) || v=ERR
      printf '%s\t%s\n' "$(gf_now)" "$v" >>"$file"
      sleep "$GF_PROBE_PERIOD"
    done
  ) &
  GF_PROBE_PID=$!
}

gf_probe_stop() { [ -n "${GF_PROBE_PID:-}" ] && kill "$GF_PROBE_PID" 2>>"$GF_LOG"; GF_PROBE_PID=""; }

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- one repetition
gf_submit_job() {
  cat <<EOF | gfk apply -f - >/dev/null
apiVersion: platform.lkhun9311.github.io/v1
kind: MLTrainingJob
metadata:
  name: $1
  namespace: $GF_NS
spec:
  queue: gpu-$GF_NS
  image: $GF_JOB_IMAGE
  command: ["sh", "-c", "trap 'exit 143' TERM; while true; do sleep 1; done"]
  gpuCount: 1
  parallelism: 1
  completions: 1
EOF
}

# gf_job_pod_on JOB NODE succeeds when the job has exactly one Pod, Ready, on NODE.
gf_job_pod_on() {
  local out
  out=$(gfk -n "$GF_NS" get pods -l "batch.kubernetes.io/job-name=$1" \
    -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.spec.nodeName}{" "}{.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Ready")].status}{"\n"}{end}') || return 1
  [ "$out" = "$2 True" ]
}

# gf_cell ARM REP BLOCK SEQ runs one registered repetition. It returns 2 when the session must stop its arms.
gf_cell() {
  local arm=$1 rep=$2 block=$3 seq=$4
  local dir field value job pre="ok" t0 t1 trb rb tend alloc0 alloc1 sentinel="" tsent=""
  dir="$GF_OUT/reps/$(printf %02d "$seq")-$arm"
  field=${GF_ARM_FIELD[$arm]} value=${GF_ARM_VALUE[$arm]} job="gf-$(printf %02d "$seq")-$arm"
  mkdir -p "$dir" || return 2
  # Written first and rewritten at the end, so a repetition the deadline cuts is scored as cut, not lost.
  printf 'mode=%s\narm=%s\nrep=%s\nblock=%s\ncut=true\n' "$GF_MODE" "$arm" "$rep" "$block" >"$dir/cell.env"
  gf_log "rep $seq: $arm (block $block, repetition $rep)"

  # Step 1: pre-state, read before anything is submitted.
  gf_is_clear || pre="target not clear before the repetition"
  local f
  for f in $GF_FIELDS; do
    [ "$(gf_readback "$f")" = 0 ] || pre="field $f not at baseline before the repetition"
  done
  # Step 2: the job on the target, with the healthy node held off until it is there.
  gfk taint node "$GF_HEALTHY" gpufault-study/hold=true:NoSchedule --overwrite >/dev/null || return 2
  gf_submit_job "$job" || { gf_log "rep $seq: could not submit $job"; return 2; }
  if ! gf_wait_until "$GF_START_S" gf_job_pod_on "$job" "$GF_TARGET"; then
    pre="the job's Pod was not Ready on $GF_TARGET within ${GF_START_S}s"
  fi
  gfk taint node "$GF_HEALTHY" gpufault-study/hold- >/dev/null || return 2
  sleep "$GF_SETTLE_S"

  gf_watch_start "$dir" "$job" || pre="a watch did not start"
  [ "$arm" != none ] && gf_probe_start "$field" "$dir/probe.tsv"
  alloc0=$(gf_allocatable)
  # Step 3: injection, bracketed by the call, then the readback that confirms it.
  t0=$(gf_now)
  if [ "$arm" != none ]; then
    gf_inject "$field" "$value" >>"$dir/inject.log" 2>&1 || gf_log "rep $seq: injection call failed"
  fi
  t1=$(gf_now)
  rb=$(gf_readback "$field" 2>>"$dir/inject.log")
  trb=$(gf_now)
  tend=$(python3 -c 'import sys; print(f"{float(sys.argv[1]) + float(sys.argv[2]):.9f}")' "$t1" "$GF_WINDOW_S")
  # Step 4: the window, from the end of the injection call.
  sleep "$(python3 -c 'import sys,time; print(max(0.0, float(sys.argv[1]) - time.time()))' "$tend")"
  if [ "$arm" = none ]; then
    # The sentinel, after the window: a benign value the operator must be seen to read, so that a quiet window
    # is a window it was watching. A distinct value per repetition, so an older reading cannot match.
    sentinel=$((100 + seq)) tsent=$(gf_now)
    gf_inject 310 "$sentinel" >>"$dir/inject.log" 2>&1
    gf_wait_until "$GF_SENTINEL_S" gf_reading_has "$GF_UUID/${GF_FIELD_NAME[310]}=$sentinel" ||
      gf_log "rep $seq: the operator did not record the sentinel within ${GF_SENTINEL_S}s"
  fi
  alloc1=$(gf_allocatable)
  gf_probe_stop
  gf_watch_stop
  {
    printf 'mode=%s\narm=%s\nrep=%s\nblock=%s\nseq=%s\njob=%s\ntarget=%s\nhealthy=%s\nuuid=%s\n' \
      "$GF_MODE" "$arm" "$rep" "$block" "$seq" "$job" "$GF_TARGET" "$GF_HEALTHY" "$GF_UUID"
    printf 'field=%s\nvalue=%s\nbaseline=0\nreadback=%s\n' "$field" "$value" "$rb"
    printf 't_inject_start=%s\nt_inject_end=%s\nt_rb=%s\nt_window_end=%s\n' "$t0" "$t1" "$trb" "$tend"
    printf 'pre_state=%s\nalloc_before=%s\nalloc_after=%s\n' "$pre" "$alloc0" "$alloc1"
    [ -n "$sentinel" ] && printf 'sentinel_value=%s\nt_sentinel_start=%s\n' "$sentinel" "$tsent"
    echo "cut=false"
  } >"$dir/cell.env"
  gfk -n "$GF_NS" get workloads.kueue.x-k8s.io,jobs.batch,pods,mltrainingjobs -o yaml >"$dir/final-objects.yaml" 2>&1
  gfk get nodehealths -o yaml >"$dir/final-nodehealths.yaml" 2>&1
  gfk -n "$GF_SYS" logs deploy/gpu-platform-control-plane-controller-manager --since=5m >"$dir/operator.log" 2>&1

  # Step 6: reset, and the job removed.
  local reset=ok
  gf_reset || reset=timeout
  gfk -n "$GF_NS" delete mltrainingjob "$job" --wait=true --timeout=60s >>"$dir/reset.log" 2>&1 || reset="$reset; job not deleted"
  gf_wait_until 60 gf_job_pods_gone "$job" || reset="$reset; pods not gone"
  echo "reset=$reset" >>"$dir/cell.env"
  gf_log "rep $seq: done, readback $rb, reset $reset"
  [ "$reset" = ok ] || return 2
}

gf_reading_has() { tr ',' '\n' <<<"$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" ann:reading)" | grep -qxF "$1"; }

# gf_job_pods_gone JOB succeeds when listing the job's Pods worked and found none; a failed list is not "none".
gf_job_pods_gone() {
  local out
  out=$(gfk -n "$GF_NS" get pods -l "batch.kubernetes.io/job-name=$1" -o name) || return 1
  [ -z "$out" ]
}

# gf_arms runs the registered twenty repetitions in their rotated blocks, stopping at the deadline, after a reset
# that does not clear, or after two invalid-looking repetitions in a row.
gf_arms() {
  local arms=(xid ecc benign none) b i arm seq=0 rc
  # Five blocks are registered; GF_BLOCKS exists only so a rehearsal can be shorter, and the scorer counts what ran.
  for b in $(seq 1 "${GF_BLOCKS:-5}"); do
    for i in 0 1 2 3; do
      arm=${arms[$(( (i + b - 1) % 4 ))]}
      seq=$((seq + 1))
      if ! grep -qx "arm_$arm=run" "$GF_OUT/smoke.env"; then
        gf_log "rep $seq: $arm is not run, by the smoke cell"
        continue
      fi
      if [ -n "${GF_ARMS_END:-}" ] && [ $(( $(date +%s) + GF_REP_BUDGET_S )) -gt "$GF_ARMS_END" ]; then
        gf_log "rep $seq and later: not started, the arms phase ends at $GF_ARMS_END"
        echo "not_started_from=$seq" >>"$GF_OUT/arms.env"
        return 0
      fi
      gf_cell "$arm" "$b" "$b" "$seq"
      rc=$?
      if [ "$rc" = 2 ]; then
        gf_log "rep $seq: the arms stop here (a reset that did not clear, or a step that could not run)"
        echo "stopped_at=$seq" >>"$GF_OUT/arms.env"
        return 0
      fi
    done
  done
}

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- smoke cell
# gf_smoke runs S1 to S6 before the operator exists, and writes smoke.env with which arms may run.
#
# The decision table is the registration's: the host engine, the exporter or field 230 or 311 failing stops the
# session, because the platform's policy needs both fault fields to call a card healthy; field 310 failing skips
# the two controls, because both use it (the benign arm injects it, and no-injection reads its sentinel).
gf_smoke() {
  local s="$GF_OUT/smoke.env" f v ok
  : >"$s"
  smoke_stop() { echo "stop=$1" >>"$s"; printf 'arm_xid=skipped\narm_ecc=skipped\narm_benign=skipped\narm_none=skipped\n' >>"$s"; gf_log "SMOKE STOP: $1"; return 1; }
  # S1.
  gf_wait_exporter || { smoke_stop "S1: no exporter serving on $GF_TARGET"; return 1; }
  if [ "$GF_MODE" = dcgm ]; then
    gfk -n "$GF_SYS" exec "$GF_POD" -c hostengine -- sh -c 'command -v dcgmi && command -v nv-hostengine' >>"$GF_OUT/smoke.log" 2>&1 ||
      { smoke_stop "S1: dcgmi or nv-hostengine not in the exporter Pod"; return 1; }
    gfk -n "$GF_SYS" exec "$GF_POD" -c hostengine -- dcgmi discovery -l >"$GF_OUT/smoke-discovery.txt" 2>&1 ||
      { smoke_stop "S1: dcgmi discovery failed"; return 1; }
    grep -q A10G "$GF_OUT/smoke-discovery.txt" || { smoke_stop "S1: the host engine does not list an A10G"; return 1; }
  fi
  echo "S1=pass" >>"$s"
  # S2: the card's UUID, and every field on /metrics.
  GF_UUID=$(gf_metrics | awk -v n="${GF_FIELD_NAME[230]}{" 'index($0, n) == 1 {match($0, /UUID="[^"]*"/); print substr($0, RSTART + 6, RLENGTH - 7); exit}')
  export GF_UUID
  [ -n "$GF_UUID" ] || { smoke_stop "S2: no UUID on ${GF_FIELD_NAME[230]}"; return 1; }
  echo "uuid=$GF_UUID" >>"$s"
  GF_FIELDS=""
  for f in 230 311 310; do
    if gf_exported "$f" >/dev/null; then echo "S2_$f=pass" >>"$s"; else echo "S2_$f=fail" >>"$s"; fi
  done
  # S3 to S5, then S6 around them.
  echo "alloc_before=$(gf_allocatable)" >>"$s"
  for f in 230 311 310; do
    v=$([ "$f" = 230 ] && echo 79 || echo 1)
    ok=pass
    grep -qx "S2_$f=pass" "$s" || ok="fail: not exported"
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && { gf_inject "$f" "$v" >>"$GF_OUT/smoke.log" 2>&1 || ok="fail: inject"; }
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && { [ "$(gf_readback "$f")" = "$v" ] || ok="fail: readback"; }
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && { gf_wait_until 10 gf_exported_is "$f" "$v" || ok="fail: not served within 10 s"; }
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && { sleep 30; gf_exported_is "$f" "$v" || ok="fail: not persistent for 30 s"; }
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && { gf_inject "$f" 0 >>"$GF_OUT/smoke.log" 2>&1 || ok="fail: reset inject"; }
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && { [ "$(gf_readback "$f")" = 0 ] || ok="fail: reset readback"; }
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && { gf_wait_until 10 gf_exported_is "$f" 0 || ok="fail: reset not served within 10 s"; }
    echo "S_inject_$f=$ok" >>"$s"
    [ "$ok" = pass ] && GF_FIELDS="$GF_FIELDS $f"
  done
  echo "alloc_after=$(gf_allocatable)" >>"$s"
  export GF_FIELDS
  grep -qx 'S_inject_230=pass' "$s" || { smoke_stop "S3: field 230"; return 1; }
  grep -qx 'S_inject_311=pass' "$s" || { smoke_stop "S4: field 311"; return 1; }
  printf 'arm_xid=run\narm_ecc=run\n' >>"$s"
  if grep -qx 'S_inject_310=pass' "$s"; then
    printf 'arm_benign=run\narm_none=run\n' >>"$s"
  else
    printf 'arm_benign=skipped\narm_none=skipped\n' >>"$s"
  fi
  gf_log "smoke: $(paste -sd' ' "$s")"
}

gf_exported_is() { [ "$(gf_exported "$1" 2>/dev/null)" = "$2" ]; }

# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ stale checks
gf_record_during() {
  local file=$1
  {
    echo "during_signal_status=$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" cond:GPUSignal.status)"
    echo "during_signal_reason=$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" cond:GPUSignal.reason)"
    echo "during_healthy_status=$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" cond:GPUHealthy.status)"
    echo "during_taint=$(gf_tainted "$GF_TARGET" && echo present || echo absent)"
    echo "during_latched=$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" ann:fingerprint)"
  } >>"$file"
}

# gf_stale_checks runs the two registered cells that take the exporter away with the operator running.
gf_stale_checks() {
  local d
  d="$GF_OUT/checks/stale-healthy"
  mkdir -p "$d"
  gf_log "check stale-healthy"
  if gf_is_clear && gf_exporter_state down; then
    sleep "$GF_STALE_WAIT_S"
    gf_record_during "$d/check.env"
    gf_exporter_state up || { gf_log "stale-healthy: the exporter did not come back"; return 1; }
    gf_wait_until 30 gf_is_clear || gf_log "stale-healthy: not clear 30 s after the exporter came back"
  else
    echo "precondition=failed" >>"$d/check.env"
  fi

  d="$GF_OUT/checks/stale-latched"
  mkdir -p "$d"
  gf_log "check stale-latched"
  gf_inject 230 79 >>"$d/inject.log" 2>&1
  gf_wait_until "$GF_LATCH_WAIT_S" gf_tainted "$GF_TARGET" || gf_log "stale-latched: no taint within ${GF_LATCH_WAIT_S}s"
  echo "before_latched=$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" ann:fingerprint)" >>"$d/check.env"
  if gf_exporter_state down; then
    sleep "$GF_STALE_WAIT_S"
    gf_record_during "$d/check.env"
    gf_exporter_state up || { gf_log "stale-latched: the exporter did not come back"; return 1; }
  fi
  # A restarted real host engine starts from an empty cache; the fake keeps its cache. The baseline is injected
  # either way, so what clears the latch is a fresh zero reading, never the exporter's return alone.
  gf_reset
  {
    echo "after_latched=$(gf_nh_field "$GF_TARGET" ann:fingerprint)"
    echo "after_taint=$(gf_tainted "$GF_TARGET" && echo present || echo absent)"
  } >>"$d/check.env"
}

# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the platform
# gf_install_queueing installs Kueue from the repository's pinned manifest, the CRDs and the resource flavor.
gf_install_queueing() {
  gfk apply --server-side -f config/kueue-controller/manifests.yaml >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || { echo "kueue apply" >&2; return 1; }
  gfk -n kueue-system wait --for=condition=Available deploy/kueue-controller-manager --timeout=300s >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 ||
    { echo "kueue not Available" >&2; return 1; }
  gfk kustomize config/crd >"$GF_OUT/crds.yaml" 2>>"$GF_LOG" || return 1
  gfk apply --server-side -f "$GF_OUT/crds.yaml" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || { echo "CRDs" >&2; return 1; }
  # Kueue's webhook answers a little after its Deployment is Available, so the flavor is retried.
  gf_wait_until 90 gf_apply_quiet config/kueue/resourceflavor.yaml || { echo "resource flavor" >&2; return 1; }
}

gf_apply_quiet() { gfk apply -f "$1" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1; }

# gf_deploy_operator IMAGE deploys the operator without its webhook, with the DCGM fault path and eviction on.
#
# Without the webhook because its certificate needs cert-manager, and nothing it guards is part of this study;
# the fault path's flags are read back from the running Deployment, not assumed from the patch.
gf_deploy_operator() {
  local img=$1 dep=gpu-platform-control-plane-controller-manager
  gfk kustomize config/operator >"$GF_OUT/operator.yaml" 2>>"$GF_LOG" || return 1
  gfk apply --server-side -f "$GF_OUT/operator.yaml" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || { echo "operator apply" >&2; return 1; }
  gfk -n "$GF_SYS" set image "deploy/$dep" "manager=$img" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || return 1
  gfk -n "$GF_SYS" patch "deploy/$dep" --type=json -p "[
    {\"op\":\"replace\",\"path\":\"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/imagePullPolicy\",\"value\":\"IfNotPresent\"},
    {\"op\":\"add\",\"path\":\"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/args/-\",\"value\":\"--gpu-fault-source=dcgm\"},
    {\"op\":\"add\",\"path\":\"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/args/-\",\"value\":\"--gpu-fault-evict-workloads\"},
    {\"op\":\"add\",\"path\":\"/spec/template/spec/containers/0/args/-\",\"value\":\"--gpu-fault-exporter-selector=$GF_EXPORTER\"}
  ]" >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || return 1
  gfk -n "$GF_SYS" rollout status "deploy/$dep" --timeout=240s >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 || { echo "operator rollout" >&2; return 1; }
  gfk -n "$GF_SYS" get "deploy/$dep" -o jsonpath='{.spec.template.spec.containers[0].args}' >"$GF_OUT/operator-args.json" || return 1
  grep -q -- '--gpu-fault-source=dcgm' "$GF_OUT/operator-args.json" || { echo "the fault path is not in the running args" >&2; return 1; }
  export GF_OPERATOR_UP=1
}

gf_cq_quota_is_one() {
  [ "$(gfk get clusterqueue "gpu-$GF_NS" -o jsonpath='{.spec.resourceGroups[0].flavors[0].resources[0].nominalQuota}')" = 1 ]
}

# gf_tenant creates the training namespace's one-GPU quota and a NodeHealth per node, then waits for both to work.
gf_tenant() {
  gfk apply -f - >>"$GF_LOG" 2>&1 <<EOF || return 1
apiVersion: v1
kind: Namespace
metadata:
  name: $GF_NS
---
apiVersion: platform.lkhun9311.github.io/v1
kind: GPUQuotaPolicy
metadata:
  name: $GF_NS-quota
spec:
  tenant: $GF_NS
  targetNamespace: $GF_NS
  limits:
    gpuCount: 1
  trainingQuota: true
---
apiVersion: platform.lkhun9311.github.io/v1
kind: NodeHealth
metadata:
  name: gf-target
spec:
  nodeName: $GF_TARGET
---
apiVersion: platform.lkhun9311.github.io/v1
kind: NodeHealth
metadata:
  name: gf-healthy
spec:
  nodeName: $GF_HEALTHY
EOF
  gf_wait_until 90 gf_cq_quota_is_one || { echo "no ClusterQueue gpu-$GF_NS with a quota of 1" >&2; return 1; }
  gf_wait_until 60 gf_is_clear || { echo "the target's NodeHealth never read clear" >&2; return 1; }
}

 succeeded in 0ms:
# The gpu-simulator DaemonSet advertises fake nvidia.com/gpu capacity on nodes with no real GPU.
#
# Design rationale (docs/09_AWS_INFRA_ARCHITECTURE.md, GPU capacity section):
# on a kind cluster a kubectl patch on the Node status is enough, because
# nothing there recomputes capacity out from under it.
#
# On EKS the kubelet owns node capacity and recomputes it continuously, so a
# patch is overwritten almost immediately.
#
# The only thing the kubelet will not overwrite is capacity it was told about
# by a registered device plugin, which is what this DaemonSet is.
#
# This profile and the real NVIDIA device plugin are mutually exclusive
# (same table): only one should be enabled at a time, toggled manually in the
# Argo CD Application.
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: DaemonSet
metadata:
  name: gpu-simulator
  namespace: system
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: gpu-platform-control-plane
    app.kubernetes.io/component: gpu-simulator
    app.kubernetes.io/managed-by: kustomize
spec:
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app.kubernetes.io/name: gpu-platform-control-plane
      app.kubernetes.io/component: gpu-simulator
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: gpu-platform-control-plane
        app.kubernetes.io/component: gpu-simulator
    spec:
      # system-node-critical keeps this pod off the eviction list under node
      # pressure, matching the convention real device plugins (including
      # NVIDIA's own) rely on to stay registered with the kubelet.
      priorityClassName: system-node-critical
      # A device plugin has to run wherever fake GPU capacity should be
      # advertised, not merely where the scheduler would place it by default.
      #
      # Tolerating everything is what makes that true regardless of which
      # taints a given node carries, including the GPU node group's own
      # nvidia.com/gpu=present:NoSchedule taint when the real node group is
      # scaled up alongside this profile during a toggle window.
      tolerations:
      - operator: Exists
      containers:
      - name: gpu-simulator
        image: gpu-simulator:latest
        command:
        - /gpu-simulator
        env:
        # FAKE_GPU_COUNT controls how many fake devices this node advertises.
        #
        # One is enough to make nvidia.com/gpu schedulable at all, which is
        # the only guarantee this simulator makes.
        - name: FAKE_GPU_COUNT
          value: "1"
        # The kubelet only accepts a device-plugin registration from a
        # process that can write into its own device-plugins directory on
        # the host, which is why this container needs more than the
        # restricted defaults the rest of config/ uses.
        #
        # privileged is broader than the socket write strictly requires, but
        # the kubelet does not expose a narrower capability for this
        # directory, so privileged is the mechanism every device plugin
        # (including NVIDIA's own) uses to get it.
        securityContext:
          privileged: true
          # The image defaults to a nonroot user, but the registration socket lives in a root-owned host directory.
          #
          # The plugin must run as root to create that socket, which is what every real device plugin does.
          runAsUser: 0
          runAsNonRoot: false
        volumeMounts:
        # hostPath, not a PVC: the kubelet looks for the registration socket
        # on the node's own filesystem, so the plugin's view of that
        # directory has to be the same directory the kubelet reads, not a
        # copy.
        - name: device-plugins
          mountPath: /var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins
        # This process holds one gRPC connection and streams a fixed device
        # list, so its resource footprint is close to idle.
        resources:
          limits:
            cpu: 100m
            memory: 64Mi
          requests:
            cpu: 10m
            memory: 32Mi
      volumes:
      - name: device-plugins
        hostPath:
          path: /var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins
          type: Directory
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 5
# The bundle of gpu-simulator manifests.
#
# config/default does not compose this directory, so nothing else rewrites
# the placeholder namespace.
#
# daemonset.yaml hardcodes namespace: system as a kubebuilder scaffold
# placeholder, matching config/gateway, and this transform rewrites that
# placeholder to the real namespace for a standalone build.
namespace: gpu-platform-control-plane-system

resources:
- daemonset.yaml
# The real NVIDIA device plugin: what makes a GPU node advertise nvidia.com/gpu capacity the kubelet will not
# overwrite.
#
# It is MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE with config/device-plugin, which is the simulator advertising fake capacity on
# nodes with no card. Both registered at once would have two plugins claiming the same resource name on the
# same node, and the lab would be measuring reservations against a number nobody owns. The simulator's own
# header says the same thing from the other side.
#
# Which one is deployed is the difference between every record reading device-not-observed and a record that
# can say otherwise, so it is a manifest rather than a step in somebody's shell history.
#
# One property is load-bearing for the verdict in internal/queuelab/device.go: this plugin allocates WHOLE
# cards. It is not configured for time-slicing, MPS, or MIG, and that is deliberate -- under any of those a
# device's utilisation is shared and the exclusivity clause refuses to attribute it, so a session configured
# that way would spend hardware to produce "not attributable" on every run. If sharing is ever the subject of
# an experiment, it needs a different verdict, not a different plugin config.
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: DaemonSet
metadata:
  name: nvidia-device-plugin
  namespace: system
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: gpu-platform-control-plane
    app.kubernetes.io/component: nvidia-device-plugin
    app.kubernetes.io/managed-by: kustomize
spec:
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app.kubernetes.io/name: gpu-platform-control-plane
      app.kubernetes.io/component: nvidia-device-plugin
  updateStrategy:
    type: RollingUpdate
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: gpu-platform-control-plane
        app.kubernetes.io/component: nvidia-device-plugin
    spec:
      # It has to run on the node whose card it is registering, which carries the taint the GPU node group
      # sets (infra/aws/cluster/eks.tf).
      tolerations:
        - key: nvidia.com/gpu
          operator: Exists
          effect: NoSchedule
      # This repository's own label, not nvidia.com/gpu.present: that one comes from GPU Feature Discovery,
      # which is not deployed here, and selecting on it would leave the plugin unschedulable and the node
      # advertising nothing -- which reads downstream as "no GPU nodes" rather than as "the plugin never ran".
      nodeSelector:
        platform.lkhun9311.github.io/gpu: "true"
      priorityClassName: system-node-critical
      containers:
        - name: plugin
          # Pinned by digest for the reason config/dcgm-exporter/daemonset.yaml gives, and resolved the same
          # way: from the registry, without a GPU node and without pulling anything. What this daemonset
          # advertises is the resource every gate in this study counts, so a tag that moved under it would
          # change what "2 allocatable" means between a qualification and the run it qualified.
          image: nvcr.io/nvidia/k8s-device-plugin:v0.16.2@sha256:ed39e22c8b71343fb996737741a99da88ce6c75dd83b5c520e0b3d8e8a884c47
          env:
            # Refuse to start rather than register a node whose driver is missing. The alternative -- starting
            # and advertising nothing -- is the failure that reads as an absent node group.
            - name: FAIL_ON_INIT_ERROR
              value: "true"
            # THE EXPERIMENT DEPENDS ON SCARCITY, so the node must advertise exactly what the protocol
            # needs and not what the instance happens to carry.
            #
            # The contrast this lab publishes is physical card contention: in every recorded run the owner
            # becomes Ready one to two seconds AFTER the victim's terminal phase, in both arms, because
            # Kueue admits the owner within 0.1 s of the preemption decision and its Pod then waits for a
            # card. The 29-second arm difference is that wait.
            #
            # The cheapest rentable instance with more than one well-supported card is a g4dn.12xlarge,
            # which carries four. Two spare devices would absorb the owner's Pod at admission in both arms,
            # the wait would collapse to a container start on each side, and the difference would fall below
            # the floor -- producing a plausible, internally consistent record set in which the study's
            # headline had been destroyed by the instance type. The preregistration's third refutation
            # condition would then read the artifact as the session's most useful discovery.
            #
            # THIS PROBABLY DOES NOT WORK, and it is left here with the reason rather than deleted, because
            # the alternative someone will reach for first is exactly this.
            #
            # It was committed as though it restricted what the plugin ADVERTISES. Two independent reviews
            # found otherwise and neither has been contradicted. NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES is read by the
            # container runtime hook to decide which already-allocated devices to inject; the plugin
            # enumerates through NVML over whatever its own container can see. This container is
            # `privileged: true`, which hands it every host device node regardless of the hook, so NVML
            # should find all four. NVIDIA/k8s-device-plugin#435 is a user setting exactly this on the
            # plugin and getting all eight GPUs still advertised; it was closed as not planned, and the
            # plugin's README documents no mechanism for restricting what it advertises.
            #
            # Nothing here has ever run against a card, so this is a prediction and not a measurement --
            # which is precisely the status the line originally claimed for itself and had not earned.
            #
            # What the session does about it is NOT this variable. hack/gpu-session.sh applies a Pod
            # labelled queuelab.gpu-platform/surplus-occupier on each worker, holding the devices the
            # protocol must not have; the run's qualification counts those out and requires what remains to
            # equal the requirement exactly. The run RECOGNISES the occupier rather than creating one -- an
            # earlier version of this comment said the run occupies the surplus itself, which is false and
            # would have sent someone looking for code that is not there.
            # all, not a list of indices.
            #
            # This said "0,1", which names two devices by index. The queuelab node (g4dn.12xlarge) has four
            # cards so both indices exist there; g5.xlarge has one A10G, so index 1 does not, and the NVIDIA
            # container runtime refuses to start the container at all:
            #
            #   failed to create the automatic CDI modifier: failed to generate CDI spec for mode "auto":
            #   failed to construct device spec generators: failed to get device handle from index: Invalid Argument
            #
            # The plugin then CrashLoopBackOff'd, nvidia.com/gpu was never advertised, and the paid session
            # refused after the card had been running for ten minutes -- reporting "the device plugin is not
            # working", which was true and did not say why.
            #
            # A device plugin has to see every device on the node it is advertising, so a fixed list is the
            # wrong shape regardless of which node it happens to fit. This is the same defect as a node group
            # name or a Region written into one file: a value that depends on the machine, carried by hand.
            - name: NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES
              value: "all"
          securityContext:
            privileged: true
          volumeMounts:
            - name: device-plugin
              mountPath: /var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins
      volumes:
        - name: device-plugin
          hostPath:
            path: /var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins
# The DCGM exporter as the GPU fault-reaction study runs it
# (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-gpu-fault-reaction-design.md).
#
# Two changes to config/dcgm-exporter, and only these, so the exporter every queuelab session used stays as it was:
#
#   1. The fault fields are collected: DCGM_FI_DEV_XID_ERRORS (230), DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_DBE_VOL_TOTAL (311) and
#      DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_SBE_VOL_TOTAL (310), beside the utilisation the queuelab reads. The exporter's default
#      counters carry the ECC totals commented out, so naming them here is what makes them exported at all.
#   2. DCGM runs as its own host engine in a second container of the same Pod, and the exporter connects to it
#      with -r localhost:5555 instead of embedding DCGM. Embedded, DCGM opens no port, and `dcgmi test --inject`
#      would have no cache to write into; with one host engine, the exporter and dcgmi read and write the same
#      cache. The host engine binds 127.0.0.1, its default, so nothing outside the Pod can reach it.
#
# The collection interval stays at -c 1000: the registration's detection bound is derived from it.
apiVersion: kustomize.config.k8s.io/v1beta1
kind: Kustomization

resources:
  - ../dcgm-exporter

patches:
  - target:
      kind: ConfigMap
      name: dcgm-exporter-metrics
    patch: |-
      - op: replace
        path: /data/queuelab-metrics.csv
        value: |
          # Format,          Prometheus metric type, help message
          DCGM_FI_DEV_GPU_UTIL, gauge, GPU utilization (in %).
          DCGM_FI_DEV_XID_ERRORS, gauge, Value of the last XID error encountered.
          DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_DBE_VOL_TOTAL, counter, Total number of double-bit volatile ECC errors.
          DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_SBE_VOL_TOTAL, counter, Total number of single-bit volatile ECC errors.
  - target:
      kind: DaemonSet
      name: dcgm-exporter
    patch: |-
      - op: add
        path: /spec/template/spec/containers/0/args/-
        value: "-r"
      - op: add
        path: /spec/template/spec/containers/0/args/-
        value: "localhost:5555"
      - op: add
        path: /spec/template/spec/containers/-
        value:
          name: hostengine
          # The same image, so the host engine and the exporter's DCGM client are one version, 3.3.9.
          image: nvcr.io/nvidia/k8s/dcgm-exporter:3.3.9-3.6.1-ubuntu22.04@sha256:3d4e0dfa5fc4d7d12689d29fc6b56cd6c610750e8d187a393882e341fbba6c12
          command: ["nv-hostengine", "-n", "--log-level", "ERROR", "-f", "/tmp/nv-hostengine.log"]
          securityContext:
            privileged: true
